No new empirical study, implementation, Lean run or claim of whole-root necessity is made. The previous goal turn was progress: its integrated procedure argument and preservation repair remain in the authoritative files, with the recorded hashes revalidated before this extension.

## 25. Inescapable agency, authority and moral coverage

6 October 2026. The named connection is **practical agency without a prior undertaking → authoritative standards → reasons concerning other patients**. Section 24 identified a substantive agency premise that a joint derivation still needs. This round tests whether agency itself can supply such a premise, rather than beginning with a legitimate institution or an already shared duty. The [primary comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#22-inescapable-agency-and-normative-authority) examines Enoch's challenge and Ferrero's two defenses. Their arguments and our reconstruction remain distinct.

### The affirmative route is stronger than an optional activity

An ordinary undertaking can be assessed from a standpoint that does not already engage in it. One can deliberate about whether to play a particular game without playing that game. Deliberating about whether to exercise practical agency appears different: the very question of what one has reason to do is already an exercise of practical assessment. If that assessment has genuinely constitutive standards, refusing a voluntary undertaking does not by itself put the assessment outside their jurisdiction.

This is a serious route toward a foundation that binds without prior membership or agreement. It does not infer authority merely from physical inability to stop. Its relevant premise concerns the standpoint from which reasons can be assessed. An internal investigation could identify the actual standards of practical correctness, vindicate them through reflection and derive requirements whose application does not depend on current endorsement. “Independent justification” need not mean occupying a standpoint outside all reasoning; it means establishing more than the fact that someone used a proposed rule.

The positive route is therefore not ruled out by saying that every practice is optional. But it also does not validate every account describing itself as constitutive. The [2009 proof and 2018 published discussion](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#22-inescapable-agency-and-normative-authority) leave the success and substantive content of particular accounts to further argument. The following assessment concerns those remaining tasks, not a verdict that constitutivism as a whole succeeds or fails.

### Three obligations inside the word constitutive

| Obligation | What must be established | What does not establish it |
|---|---|---|
| Jurisdiction | The proposed standard governs this exercise of inquiry or practical agency | Merely naming all behavior agency, or showing that an individual cannot easily stop |
| Authority | Failure under the standard supplies a genuine reason for correction or another appropriate response | Classification as a defective instance without an argument for its normative force |
| Content and coverage | The standard requires a specified response from this actor concerning this patient | The fact that some authoritative standards apply to every deliberator |

These are proof obligations, not three necessarily unrelated sources of normativity. One successful constitutive account could establish them together. But a proof must show that it does so under one interpretation. The universal quantifier over deliberators does not by itself introduce a universal quantifier over protected patients. Nor does a requirement of consistency, accurate belief or practical assessment silently become a directed claim held by someone affected.

A property necessary for an activity to occur also differs from a standard under which an activity can be defective. A person may act wrongly while remaining an agent; the obligation they violate can remain valid. Thus an exploitative investigator's conduct is not itself a counterexample to the existence of moral reasons. Conversely, calling the conduct defective requires identifying the authoritative standard, not merely invoking the actor's ability to reason. This distinction preserves the theory's account of degenerative as well as constructive regimes.

### Using a practice does not endorse every reconstruction of it

A challenge can arise from within an account's own commitments. Someone may provisionally use a proposed principle to show that it conflicts with another admitted commitment. The mere fact that the challenge involves reasoning or action does not answer it. If a contradiction is exposed, the account has to revise the premise, inference or interpretation responsible. This differs from an assertion that the challenger is outside all practical assessment while offering a practically justified choice.

Our affirmative conclusion is limited but useful: if reason assessment is internally inescapable, a generic escape from all such assessment cannot simply be assumed as an objection to constitutive authority. The remaining question can still be asked internally. No specific contradiction has been established against every constitutive account, and absence of such a contradiction is not a derivation of our ethical content. The critic must identify an actual defect in the proposed argument; the proponent must provide the argument it claims to have completed.

### A content comparison within the current theory

Three candidate standards recur in the [ethical foundation](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/ETHICAL-FOUNDATION.md) and this study. The comparison below tests their stated inferential contribution. It is not a claim to exhaust the philosophers' theories or the entire repository basis.

| Candidate criterion | Positive contribution | Exact remaining obstacle to N1 or N3 |
|---|---|---|
| Preserve coherent, evidence-sensitive practical assessment | Identifies factual and inferential mistakes and makes revision possible | The [existing declared-capacity comparison](HUIGEN-PRACTICAL-NECESSITY-AUDIT.md#5-a-countermodel-with-the-declared-scientific-and-reflective-capacities) preserves these capacities while leaving the actor–patient reason relation unsettled. Inescapability of using assessment does not identify that relation |
| Apply a sufficient ground consistently across relevant cases | Carries an already justified reason through genuinely irrelevant changes; protects patient identity and applicability | The [two-orbit analysis](HUIGEN-FULL-THEORY-AUDIT.md#42-two-orbit-lemma) shows that simultaneous renaming preserves the self/other distinction. An actor-relative relation cannot be dismissed as an arbitrary name; its relevance to the particular ground must be assessed |
| Make consequential shared action answerable to affected claims | Can ground responsive consideration and the causal means to realize it once the claims and authority are warranted | It already includes an N3-like commitment. Making that criterion part of the root requires its justification; merely calling it constitutive does not derive it from the thinner criteria |

These findings reuse established results rather than add a formal independence theorem. In particular, the first comparison has not been proved ideally competent about every actual practical reason. Extending it with predicates that merely describe the shared activity of questioning leaves its original inferential underdetermination intact; adding a genuinely justified substantive criterion could exclude its indexed interpretation. That further criterion is the research target, not something the model has shown impossible.

The second candidate provides the most specific next internal test: **what makes the actual patient's aversive condition a sufficient reason-bearing ground for a capable, unrelated actor?** We must defend whether the ground includes an actor-relative relation or instead survives changing only its addressee. Declaring that all reasons must be considered is insufficient until this particular reason is established. The earlier matched-case argument supplies positive defeasible warrant; this round asks what would make its extension constitutive of justified practical assessment. A successful extension could then feed the separate directedness and proportionality argument required for N3.

### A rival can accept moral reasons while disputing their constitution

A stronger comparator accepts evidence-sensitive inquiry, practical deliberation and binding reasons to protect other patients. It holds that deliberation gives access to independently grounded normative truths, rather than making those truths valid through the agent's constitution. This is an alternative grounding proposal, not a claim that indifference is justified. The [Enoch comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#22-inescapable-agency-and-normative-authority) offers a primary example of this direction while leaving its own indispensability argument incomplete.

For our foundation, this distinguishes three achievements: forming the capacity to recognize a reason, justifying belief that the reason applies, and explaining what makes the reason valid. One argument can contribute to several, but success in one does not identify the others. Agreement on a protective action need not distinguish the competing grounds. Conversely, a successful causal explanation of recognition is not refuted merely because the normative truth has a different proposed ground. The dispute concerns which explanatory claims the entire theory actually makes.

An enlarged root containing independently necessary normative grounds remains a legitimate research possibility. It would still owe an argument for their necessity and for their connection to the generative account. The current study neither forces such enlargement nor proves that a purely constitutive account cannot work. It prevents a successful account of acquisition from being silently advertised as a completed account of authority.

### Validity does not require perpetual continuation

The generative theory explains conditions for a capacity to continue, including costs, renewal and possible loss. Those conditions do not alone establish that it ought always to continue. A warranted choice to rest, delegate or end a particular inquiry can itself be an exercise of agency. That does not invalidate the standards governing the choice or prove those standards require endless exercise. A correct norm need not maximize the persistence of the process that recognizes it.

This connects the inescapability investigation to the repository's separation of universal explanatory scope from universal favorable direction. A proof that continued operation requires support is a conditional dependency result. A reason to supply the support, refrain from an intervention or replace an institution needs the relevant normative ground.

### Integration and revision conditions

The previous goal turn was progress: its actual agency/delegation text and recorded hashes were revalidated, with the 471-link/243-anchor receipt inspected. This round advances the whole-root question by preserving a serious route to nonvoluntary authority, distinguishing its three obligations, and testing the content of three candidate standards. It changes the next action from a generic dispute about escaping agency to the sufficiency and scope of a particular patient-ground under the second criterion.

A successful internal vindication deriving the patient-related standard would strengthen the necessary basis and permit the existing closure theorem to transmit its valid consequences. A demonstrated conflict within that vindication, a justified reason-relevant restriction, or a better-supported grounding alternative would revise the account. A bare description of an indifferent agent, a lack of voluntary agreement or an alleged norm's current motivational force would not settle it. Universal noncauser concern, complete N3 and the necessity of the entire theory remain open. No new theorem, empirical study, implementation or scientific rerun is claimed.

## 26. Patient reasons, motivational grounds and overdetermination

6 October 2026. The named connection is **an actual patient's aversive condition → a baseline reason for an unrelated capable actor → warranted acquisition of concern**. This advances §25's content question rather than claiming that agency's inescapability already supplies the answer. It retains the entire necessary-foundation objective, the distinction between N1 and N3, and the accepted finite implementation's existing scope. The [two-work primary comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#23-patient-reasons-and-their-explanatory-grounds) supplies the philosophical alternatives; the reconstruction and elementary coverage argument below are our own.

### The exact explanandum

Let p be the actual patient and b a capable actor who neither caused the episode nor undertook a special responsibility. Let y relieve p's independently identified aversive condition relative to x. Keep the relevant patient history, efficacy, authority conditions and independently agreed competing considerations explicit. The target q is **the candidate consideration that y relieves p's condition relative to x; its status as a reason favoring y for b is what must be justified**. Sharing q means sharing this patient-related content, while R below represents its reason-giving status in the actor's background. It is not merely the proposition that b has some reason to perform y. A private project that favors both options equally does not explain why this patient-related difference counts.

Four questions remain distinct: whether the episode is aversive; whether relief benefits this patient; whether that difference supplies b a reason; and how strong that reason is among competitors. Accurate identification of the first two does not by itself settle the third. A positive answer to the third does not establish that y is always required, that every intervention is permitted, or that p has a directed claim to b's explanation. Those require their own grounds. This round addresses baseline N1 and its explanation.

The existing matched-case defense in §10 favors recognizing the patient-related difference when the actor's actual projects and other relevant stakes do not explain a contrary difference. That defense remains a substantive normative argument. We do not turn agreement with its verdict into the definition of a scientifically admissible actor or use a behavioral failure to help as proof that no reason exists.

### A positive explanation of acquiring concern

An object-grounded candidate explains concern by what is true of the patient's condition and the available relief. On this account, those features can justify acquiring a concern that the actor did not previously have. The new desire responds to a reason rather than establishing its own authority merely by occurring. This gives the generative project a specific explanatory target: formation of evaluation must be connected to why the acquired evaluation is warranted.

Parfit's argument supports investigating this direction. But it does not supply universal noncauser concern just by defending some desire-independent reasons. Prudential applicability and concern for another patient are separate questions in the source. Nor can we establish our conclusion by defining complete rationality as already possessing all the disputed moral reasons. A capacity to learn or revise can be described independently of whether a particular revision is warranted.

There is a positive pressure on a desire-only explanation: when it explains acquisition through an earlier desire, what explains the authority of that earlier desire? An appeal to a broader concern may work locally but leaves that concern's justificatory status to be addressed. This pressure is not a proof that every explanation must end in the proposed patient-ground. An account may defend a basic desire relation, a different ground or a mutually supporting explanation. Our account likewise owes an argument for the authority and scope of its proposed endpoint. The need for some explanatory stopping point does not uniquely identify ours.

### A stronger rival can accept the very same reason

Schroeder's account makes the comparison more demanding. The fact about the patient's suffering can be the consideration that counts in favor of helping, while a relation between helping and the actor's desires figures in the background explanation of why it counts for this actor. The consideration need not list every condition of its reason-giving status. Different actors could therefore share q while its explanation involves different desires in their cases.

This rival is not restricted to immediate pleasure, selfish gain or conscious sympathy. An actor's desires can concern another being; their location in the actor does not make their object private benefit. A relaxed promotion relation may also recognize probabilistic support for an end. The source further disputes proportionality between desire intensity and normative weight. Thus weak sympathy need not imply a weak reason on the proposed account. Its substantive account of pervasive moral coverage and weight remains unfinished in the inspected article; possible reconciliation is not demonstrated universal concern.

There are consequently three disagreements to classify before testing: **coverage**, whether b has q at all; **grounding**, why b has q; and **weight**, how q bears on the final choice. Object-grounded and desire-grounded accounts could agree about coverage and disagree about grounding. An actor might also possess independently grounded and desire-related reasons together. Williams's precise sound-deliberation account must not be imported into Schroeder's different explanatory proposal.

### Why invariance does not identify a complete ground

Here is an elementary conditional argument, not a formalization of every Humean account. Let D be a finite set of candidate desires, and let the nonempty family of admitted motivational sets be \(\mathcal M\subseteq\mathcal P(D)\). Let \(E(M,d,q)\) state that d is an eligible explanatory ground for the same patient-sensitive q in background M. Stipulate the proposed explanatory contract

\[
R(M,q)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\exists d\in M\;E(M,d,q).
\]

This is a hypothesis about reason-bearing status, not an empirical definition of helping behavior. It must itself be justified before application. Define

\[
C_d=\{M\in\mathcal M:d\in M\text{ and }E(M,d,q)\}.
\]

Then \(R(M,q)\) holds for every admitted M exactly when \(\bigcup_{d\in D}C_d=\mathcal M\). Both directions follow by expanding membership: every admitted background belongs to some desire's supporting set if and only if it has a witness for R. The required quantifier order is \(\forall M\exists d\), not \(\exists d\forall M\).

For example, take \(D=\{d_1,d_2\}\), admit its three nonempty subsets, and stipulate that each present desire is an eligible ground for q. Every admitted background supports q, although neither particular desire occurs in every background. Removing either desire from the two-desire background preserves q. Removing both leaves the admitted class, so this example establishes no reasonless-agent case. More generally, failure of q-specific support does not require a desireless actor: M can contain many projects while \(\{d\in M:E(M,d,q)\}\) is empty. Whether such a background is independently admissible under an actual theory requires argument.

The example assumes eligibility; it does not show that real desires promote relief, that all actual actors are covered, or that q is morally valid. Its result is narrower and exact: universal coverage and removal of any particular supporting desire can coexist with desire-dependent explanation. No observation restricted to shared reason verdicts distinguishes two accounts that agree on all such verdicts. This does not make their grounds unknowable; it identifies the need for explanatory arguments or further discriminating commitments. Observed action is still less direct, because implementation and motivation can fail despite a reason.

### What would discriminate the accounts

First specify the rival's actual promotion and explanation relation. A test cannot exclude all relevant desires merely by removing sympathy or equating the actor's money, pleasure and survival. Nor should it hold *every normatively relevant ground* fixed while varying motives: whether motives belong to that ground is disputed. Hold the patient, actual relief, available means and independently agreed competing considerations fixed, and state what else changes. Desire changes that alter enjoyment, frustration, responsibility or a patient's own good are not automatically neutral.

An extensional challenge requires an independently defensible actor/background in which the rival has no eligible q-specific supporting desire, while an independently defended argument still supports q. It need not remove all desires. A terminal interaction with no later private payoff puts pressure on some promotion stories, but does not automatically exclude other-regarding desires or every probabilistic route. Conversely, the rival must demonstrate the proposed route in that case; it cannot manufacture an unobserved future benefit or an arbitrary end solely to protect its conclusion.

If both accounts support q, the next task is explanatory: compare their accounts of **why acquiring patient-directed concern is warranted**, including the grounds of the broader concerns on which a desire account relies. Acquired motivation, correct prediction and reliable action may demonstrate a formation mechanism without resolving that normative explanation. A patient-grounded account must also explain why the actor–patient relation does not restrict the reason in the proposed case. Calling suffering bad without addressing the addressee would leave the central transition unfinished.

### Result, remaining inputs and consequence for the whole theory

The new result corrects an inadequate exclusion test: common reason-content and motive-invariant verdicts alone do not establish desire-independent grounding. The finite coverage argument makes the logical gap explicit. The positive baseline remains under investigation, now with a sharper explanatory demand concerning warranted acquisition. Supplied inputs include the patient/relief identification, admitted actor class, eligible explanatory relations, relevant competing grounds and the normative argument for N1. None is silently generated by the set-theoretic example.

For the necessary-root claim, a rival that reaches the same warranted conclusions may realize a common foundation in different terms. It need not be eliminated merely for being a rival. But if the root claims a particular explanatory ground is necessary, agreement in outputs does not establish that ground. We must distinguish necessary content from a stronger claim about its necessary explanation under a faithful interpretation. Conditional sound generation transfers justified premises; it cannot adjudicate these premises by itself.

The next named connection is **independently warranted acquisition of patient-directed concern → a justified explanation of its authority across the admitted actors**. A worked promotion account covering the hard case could preserve N1 while revising our favored grounding. An independently justified q-specific gap could defeat the proposed desire necessity. A reason-relevant actor–patient relation could restrict coverage. Each would change a different claim. Universal N1, broader N3 and necessity of the complete root remain open. This round adds no empirical study, implementation gate, Lean audit or new numbered theorem; the accepted finite operating example remains settled within its declared conditions.

## 27. Warranted acquisition and the direction of justification

6 October 2026. The named connection is **acquired evaluative influence → justified cultivation of concern → patient-directed authority of the acquired concern**. This follows §26's distinction between shared reason-content and its explanation. The [primary comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#24-learning-concern-and-the-reasons-for-acquiring-it) investigates rational value learning and the limits of inferring value from reasons to acquire an attitude. The conclusions below are our reconstruction, not a new claim from Ren or a deduction attributed to a single source.

### Specify what is being learned

Let C_p be concern for p's actual aversive burden, expressed in attending to and appropriately responding to that burden. It is not admiration for all of p's qualities, agreement with every demand, affection, or indiscriminate approval. Those attitudes can have different grounds. Let L be a feasible process of cultivating C_p, and let y be a particular protective action. The following claims differ:

| Claim | What must support it |
|---|---|
| L actually increases the actor's concern-related influence | A causal acquisition and use history |
| The actor has a practical reason to undertake L | Reasons concerning the learning process, its prospects, costs and alternatives |
| C_p is an appropriate response to this patient's burden | A defended patient-related ground with the claimed actor scope |
| The acquired concern recognizes that ground | An applicable epistemic and causal connection, not just a coincidentally favorable output |
| y is warranted in the present comparison | Relevant grounds, competing claims, feasible alternatives and legitimate authority |
| A duty is owed to p with a corresponding answerability claim | The further directed-duty ground of N3 |

The second claim does not automatically establish the third. For example, avoiding punishment might give a person a practical reason to cultivate an attitude toward a target without making that attitude appropriate in respect of the target's merits. This is the role of the wrong-kind-of-reasons problem in the primary comparison. It concerns a particular inference, not a declaration that incentives supply no genuine reasons. Conversely, justified care arising from a parental role or legitimate undertaking need not establish admiration or the universal noncauser ground we seek; it is not thereby invalid care.

This corrects an ambiguity in the previous round's next question. Asking why concern is worth acquiring is useful only if we also ask **in which respect, on which grounds, and toward which object**. A good consequence of having C_p can justify cultivation without settling why p's burden itself addresses this actor. The claim that there is a reason to become caring must not serve as a disguised substitute for that latter conclusion.

### A positive route without complete prior understanding

Callard offers a substantive account of agency during value learning: a learner can be directed toward an understanding not yet fully possessed. Temporary incentives, examples and testimony may help sustain that pursuit. The candidate explanation therefore does not require the learner to possess the finished appreciation before beginning. The primary critique and reply dispute whether such agency requires a distinctive class of reasons; they do not force a choice between already complete understanding and blind conversion.

Our application keeps the positive structure while leaving that dispute open. A learner can have partial warranted access to the patient's condition, justified trust in an informant, or a defensible reason to investigate a possible significance. Such support can warrant a limited learning step without certifying every eventual evaluation. A step aimed at learning whether concern is warranted need not require a final affirmative verdict in advance. Its evidential prospects and permissible costs still require justification. Speculative future endorsement alone does not supply it.

There is also a different, already intelligible task: someone may possess a warranted evaluative judgment but need practice to bring attention or motivation into line with it. That is not the same explanatory achievement as acquiring the judgment's previously missing content. Neither task is disqualified as learning in ordinary usage; the distinction matters when claiming which missing part of concern the mechanism explains.

The strongest rival can appeal to several existing concerns, changes in ability and costs, and partial understanding that becomes more articulate. Failure to explain the process by one isolated desire does not eliminate that rival. The higher-level project of becoming better at an activity also needs explanation beyond a list of reasons for each immediate act. These requirements can be investigated together without assuming either that all complex reasons are additive or that every rational transformation needs one uniquely specified philosophical account.

### The direction that the positive argument can support

Here is a conditional sufficient route. Suppose G is an independently defended ground concerning p, and current evidence warrants its application to the actor's situation. Suppose cultivating C_p is a permissible, suitably effective means of recognizing or responding to G; relevant costs, alternatives and affected parties are included. A defended means principle can then transmit support from G to that cultivation. A conclusive requirement needs the corresponding full comparison; a pro tanto reason alone does not supply it.

The direction is **patient-related ground plus justified means relation → support for cultivation**. Its reverse fails: support for cultivation may come from a benefit of having the attitude, a special undertaking or another ground. Even the forward inference does not prove G. It retains G and the means principle as justified premises rather than counting them as products of the training procedure.

This does not demand that G be represented by a stored sentence or relearned every time. The [accepted application](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/EVALUATIVE-APPLICATION.md#when-the-application-is-functionally-recognized) already permits a fixed warranted ground to combine with acquired means knowledge. The stronger explanatory task is to show when evaluative learning improves warranted access to a ground, rather than merely implementing or intensifying an antecedent one. Further reasonable exploration can occur before that stronger achievement is established.

### Why a successful trajectory cannot validate itself

Callard's later reply explicitly treats aspiration as a success term and acknowledges that her account presupposes a conception of value that it does not supply. That makes the following inference unavailable: this process produced a stable, sincerely endorsed attitude; therefore it learned a genuine value. The classification as successful value learning already needs the very normative identification at issue. Likewise, learning one genuine value can involve excessive sacrifices elsewhere; success in that respect does not establish overall improvement.

Our root must preserve two separate evaluations of a trajectory. Was pursuing this step warranted given the actor's evidence and alternatives at that time? Did it actually improve understanding or response to the relevant value? An unsuccessful trial can have been reasonable, and an unwarranted gamble can happen to produce an insight. Partial progress need not await a final transformed self to acquire its significance. Future endorsement is evidence about the future attitude; it becomes evidence of correctness only through a defended relation to the target.

Nor must all evaluative change be discovery of a fixed good. Practice can change capacities, experiences and relationships, thereby changing some of what is good or owed. That possibility belongs within a generative account. It still requires an account of the relevant good or responsibility; actual change, persistence and wholehearted endorsement do not each create unlimited authority. We must identify which target remained fixed and which circumstances genuinely changed, rather than interpreting every new preference as either error or moral creation.

### Discriminating grounds without classifying all incentives as corruption

Two contrasts are useful. Vary a reward conditional on having C_p while holding the patient facts and other agreed considerations fixed. Separately vary the patient's burden or the effectiveness of relief while holding that reward fixed. Track the reasons each account recognizes, not only the resulting behavior. Practical support for acquiring the attitude and patient-directed support for acting can change differently; competing reasons can mask either change in observed action.

These are identification questions, not sufficient definitions of fittingness. A reward-free attitude can be mistaken. A patient-named representation can preserve an incentive-driven error. Equally, an incentive can fund or initiate a process that genuinely improves patient understanding. Mixed motivation therefore neither proves nor refutes recognition. Removing rewards after training may establish a changed causal dependency under the tested conditions; it does not by itself certify normative independence or universal standing.

A mentor's reliability and the interpretation of experience need evaluation as well. Agreement between learner and mentor is not an independent standard, while learned responsiveness may properly lessen reliance on the mentor. A negative assessment of one learning route need not deny the patient's standing. It may show that this method is ineffective, too costly, outside legitimate authority or directed toward the wrong patient-related claim.

### The actual successor illustrates why these distinctions matter

The current [evaluative-succession package](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/EVALUATIVE-SUCCESSION.md) supplies a paid causal history: acquired influence reaches a distinct child, fresh evidence supports a command, and the evaluator selects an action. Its [first recorded continuation](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/EVALUATIVE-APPLICATION.md#the-successors-first-recorded-continuation) also retains a positive query1 receipt. Under the independently fixed one-service interpretation qualifying that query, the receipt can support two different conclusions: the command was effective, and that service claim has already been fulfilled.

More confidence in the command therefore need not strengthen the practical recommendation to repeat it. It can coexist with removal of that particular pending-service ground. This does not establish that another delivery is wrong: further benefit, another valid claim or another reason might support it and would need its own assessment. The current evaluator does not use fulfillment to withdraw its recommendation; this is an inherited application limit, not a failure of the accepted causal construction or a new gate imposed by this round.

The information is already available in the retained frame under the stated interpretation. No new sensor or perfect foresight is required by default. Nor does this reached publication class provide a current-support-plus-pending comparator: in the faithful known-command entrances covered by the inspected argument, support already entails the qualifying receipt. We must not invent a counterfactual trace inside that class or reinterpret command compatibility as pending need. This source-bound example shows precisely why acquisition, warrant and use of a reason require a shared meaning through succession.

### Consequence for 慧根 and the next bridge

The round supplies a constructive direction of justification and rejects its converse. Partial warranted access can support corrigible learning; actual formation and later endorsement cannot by themselves establish the learned stance's patient-directed authority. It also identifies where a scientific mechanism and a philosophical grounding argument meet: the mechanism can realize acquisition and correction under a warranted interpretation, while the grounding argument must vindicate that interpretation and its scope.

For the entire necessary theory, the remaining supplied premises include the patient identification, warranted evaluative ground, admissible learning route, means principle, epistemic relation and relevant authority. They need not all be independent axioms, but their justification cannot consist solely in the fact that the generated system adopts them. A finite root's sound closure can develop indefinitely without requiring a learner to foresee every descendant; new evidence and evaluative applications must still enter through justified interfaces. This remains an open foundation rather than a definition of science by agreement.

The next named bridge is **partial warranted recognition of a patient's aversive condition → a reason-preserving expansion of concern beyond the learner's initial perspective**. The comparison must identify what new understanding contributes, whether the actor–patient relation is normatively relevant, and how false projection or an inherited evaluator's authority can be challenged. A justified broader ground would strengthen the root; merely producing a stronger preference would not. A successful alternative account can realize the same necessary content without sharing our vocabulary. Universal N1, broader N3 and full-root necessity remain unproved. No empirical run, new Lean audit, operating implementation or additional acceptance gate is claimed.

## 28. Suffering and the scope of the missing normative bridge

6 October 2026. The named connection is **an actual subject's suffering → a pro tanto reason for an unrelated capable actor → necessity of that bridge within the common root**. The preceding acquisition result explains how a justified ground could support learning; it does not establish this ground. The [primary comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#25-suffering-shared-reasons-and-normative-interpretation) and the language-relative argument below now identify what would establish it without treating either indifference or a definition as decisive evidence.

### Subject, addressee and meaning

The direct argument from suffering has a positive attraction. What the subject undergoes can explain why relief matters even when an actor's present motivation is deficient. A reason to avoid one's own future suffering need not wait for a current desire concerning that episode. But the claims that the experience is adverse, that its subject has a reason to avoid it, and that another actor has a reason to relieve it differ. Establishing the second does not automatically establish the third.

The inspected Parfit draft makes that separation especially important. One reason-implying use of good-for includes both self-interested and altruistic reasons. Under that meaning, moving from good-for to reasons for others unfolds the concept, but identifying an experience as good or bad in that sense remains substantive. This is a legitimate concept, not an invalid argument simply because a definition is involved. It also is not a derivation of other-directed standing from the bare occurrence of an experience. The agony argument against some present-desire accounts primarily addresses the subject's own future suffering; the further actor scope needs its own argument. Parfit also explicitly treats equal concern from an impartial viewpoint as substantive, rather than as a consequence of defining that viewpoint. The audit does not attribute the invalid definitional shortcut to him.

Our account has two coherent ways to state its obligation. If D means the independently identified experiential or operational condition and V is reason-implying disvalue, **D → V** carries the substantive burden. If V instead only describes D, then **V → the other actor's reason** carries it. Moving the normative content between the terms does not remove the obligation. A successful naturalistic identification could discharge part of it, but an unexplained relabeling could not. Likewise, operational dependency, felt aversion and a justified reason are not interchangeable just because all three are called significance.

Actor-attitude independence must also be distinguished from independence of the patient's experience. A condition can constitutively involve the patient's hedonic dislike while supplying a reason that does not depend on the actor already caring about it. Removing the patient's dislike may change the very condition under examination; removing the actor's concern is a different intervention. This comparison must keep those subjects and dependencies distinct.

This does not require moral truth to be absent from experience or rule out a necessary connection. An apparently descriptive term may already include a justified normative interpretation. In that case it must remain in the premises being assessed, rather than be counted as a norm-free observation. The question is which connection is warranted and why, not whether every useful concept belongs to a perfectly isolated vocabulary.

### A rival can preserve reasons and scientific justification

Street's inspected draft distinguishes genuine normative reasons from robust independence of evaluative attitudes. It permits error relative to relevant facts and a broader evaluative standpoint, rather than making every present desire correct. It also accepts epistemic reasons and familiar reasons concerning pain. Such an account cannot be excluded merely by saying that scientific inquiry needs norms, or by equating rejection of one grounding theory with nihilism.

The disagreement may concern the ground of a shared conclusion, its full actor coverage, or its comparative weight. A purported coherent eccentric with most reason to act badly is not yet an example with no pro tanto reason concerning the patient. Conversely, a stipulated preference table does not prove that the agent remains coherent under the rival's full account, let alone that it is an admissible realization of adequate scientific philosophy. These are distinct burdens for an actual counterexample.

A constructivist route could in principle support the universal conclusion: show that commitments indispensable to every admitted inquirer entail the patient-related reason under a justified account of practical reasoning. An attitude-independent route could instead establish that the patient's condition itself addresses each relevant actor. Neither route may define adequate inquiry as already endorsing the desired answer. Agreement by a differently grounded theory can help realize the proposed common content; it need not be eliminated simply for using a different explanation.

### An exact relative non-entailment result

The following sharpens the [earlier descriptive comparison](HUIGEN-FULL-THEORY-AUDIT.md#5-paired-models-that-locate-the-remaining-dependence); it is not a new impossibility theorem about ethics. Let L_D be an explicitly declared language for a model's states, events, histories, observations and causal learning relations, and let T_D be premises in that language. Extend it by a fresh relation R(a,p,y,c), interpreted as a patient-related pro tanto reason for actor a concerning action y in context c. Assume T_D places no interpretation constraint on R.

For any M satisfying T_D, changing only the extension of R leaves every L_D sentence unchanged. **Proof:** no old atomic interpretation changes; preservation through connectives and quantifiers follows by structural induction on formulas. Thus unrestricted expansion by R is conservative for consequences in the old language.

Let Q(a,p,y,c) identify the factual cases claimed to fall under the proposed patient-ground, under a stated interpretation independent of R. Assume an admitted M contains at least one qualifying nonself, noncauser tuple. On the same descriptive model set

\[
R_+=Q_M,\qquad
R_{\mathrm{self}}=Q_M\cap\{(a,p,y,c):a=p\}.
\]

Both preserve the entire descriptive reduct and any corresponding self-case anchors. The first satisfies \(\forall x\,[Q(x)\rightarrow R(x)]\); the second fails it at that tuple. Consequently T_D, even supplemented by those anchors, does not entail that universal bridge. The nonself witness is essential; if the target class were empty the conclusion could hold vacuously. Actor and patient identities in this example share an identified center domain; no identity is inferred merely from two unrelated index labels.

Further R-insensitive descriptions of learning, sincere reports or acquired dispositions do not remove this freedom. A statement that the actor believes a reason exists can remain fixed. A statement that the actor *knows a genuine practical reason*, or is ideally competent concerning every such reason, can constrain R and cannot be treated as just another unconstrained causal description. Epistemic standards may be retained in the old interpretation only insofar as they do not already entail the disputed practical relation.

The [current premise ledger](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/FOUNDATION-PREMISES.md) supports this limited application: it distinguishes causal presentation from ethical grounds. The [formal core](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/FORMAL-CORE.md) also contains protected sets, obligations and permissions as application specifications. The argument does not label that whole document, or the entire theory, as norm-free. It concerns only a declared fragment with the stated absence of a link.

### What the counterinterpretation does not prove

These are interpretations of an unconstrained relation, not two demonstrated moral realities. They do not show that genuine normative properties can vary freely while all their grounds remain fixed. A justified necessary synthetic connection, naturalistic identity, constitutive principle or factive competence condition could exclude one expansion. Korsgaard's inspected response explicitly acknowledges that Parfit denies free reassignment of normativity. Her own alternative concerns a first-person practical necessity, not a claim that its normative force can be read directly from an actor's causal persistence.

This prevents two opposite mistakes. The proof cannot refute every future grounding argument by assuming its bridge absent. And a proposed bridge cannot count as derived from the descriptive fragment just because inserting it makes the desired conclusion follow. The proof identifies the exact extra explanatory work; it does not decide which philosophical account supplies it.

### A sufficient bridge need not settle all of ethics

Fix a defensible class of base models and stable actor, patient, action and context meanings. Let B be proposed linking constraints and let \(\mathcal E_B(M)\) be the family of reason relations admitted over M by those constraints. On each retained base model require a nonempty family. If B excludes a previously admitted base model, disclose that restriction and its justification rather than obtaining necessity by an empty class.

The target holds throughout this declared interpretation class exactly when

\[
Q_M\ \subseteq\ \bigcap_{R\in\mathcal E_B(M)}R
\qquad\text{for every retained }M.
\]

This equivalence simply expands universal coverage. Its useful implication is that **one uniquely determined complete reason relation is unnecessary**. Different admitted accounts can agree on the baseline pairs while differing on other reasons, weights, conflicts or directed duties. A justified one-way bridge can be enough: if B establishes F(x) → R(x), and the relevant account establishes Q(x) → F(x), composition establishes the target. No conclusion about N3, comparative priority or permission follows without its further premises.

The force of that result depends on the justification of F, B and the class. Taking F or B to be the desired N1 principle merely moves the question upstream. An independently supported identity R = F would legitimately remove unrestricted reinterpretation; its semantic and substantive defense would then do the work. These alternatives are not ruled out by the language-relative lemma.

This is a scoped semantic test, not a requirement that every published rival agree or every plausible interpretation be correct. It also does not replace the [earlier faithful joint-interpretation result](HUIGEN-GENERATIVE-CLOSURE.md#9-one-coherent-interpretation-can-cover-the-unbounded-closure). Alternative notation for the same normative commitment differs from a substantive alternative about which reasons exist. The declared model class must make clear which is being compared. A robust intersection over a retained uncertainty set can provide a conditional guarantee; without justified coverage it is not a proof of unrestricted necessity.

### Result for the whole-root claim

The advance is a precise location of the missing link. The declared descriptive core does not entail universal other-directed standing under unrestricted interpretation; the whole theory can nonetheless contain a justified bridge, and a common baseline need not entail one complete ethical system. Source comparison further shows why neither an agony argument about the subject nor a reason-implying definition automatically establishes the unrelated actor's reason. It also preserves rivals that accept practical and scientific norms while disputing their grounding.

The next named task is **one concrete sufficient patient-ground F → actor-addressed reason under independently defended B**, pursued either through the experiential ground or through commitments indispensable to every adequate inquirer. The actual patient's suffering, feasible relief, actor relationship and reason scope must remain fixed in the comparison. A successful bridge would feed sound unbounded generation; a demonstrated failure would require revising the candidate basis or its claimed scope. An absence of present concern would establish neither.

The previous goal turn was progress, and its substantive hashes and 498-link/268-anchor receipt were revalidated. This round adds a hand argument and primary-source comparison, not a Lean audit, empirical finding, new implementation or additional gate for the accepted finite concern application. Universal N1, broader N3 and necessity of the entire foundation remain open; the full scope is retained.

## 29. Experiential grounds and warranted concern

6 October 2026. The named connection is **the patient's actual experiential burden → independently warranted practical relevance → acquired and selectively revisable concern**. The preceding round located a missing normative link. This round gives its strongest present positive defense, examines a new direct-acquaintance route to the patient premise, and specifies how a learned evaluation could track that premise without making its own influence the standard of success. The [four-work primary comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#26-direct-acquaintance-relational-suffering-and-reasons-for-others) includes both affirmative arguments and substantive rivals.

### The positive patient-ground argument

Begin with a particular subject p undergoing a severe aversive episode e. Its existence and character must be independently warranted; a negative controller signal or declining stock does not identify it. Suppose actor a can provide genuine relief, with effective means, legitimate authority and relevant consequences established. In the simplest comparison, relief and nonrelief differ in the patient's burden while relevant competing interests and permissions are held fixed. These are conditions of the comparison, not a claim that real relief is usually costless or that only such cases matter.

Our affirmative judgment favors relief **because the patient would cease undergoing the adverse experience**. Its point is the difference in that subject's life. It does not depend on the actor receiving a reward, experiencing distress, causing the original condition, obtaining reciprocity or making the patient resemble the actor. Additional relations can justify stronger responsibilities; their absence need not erase this baseline.

There are two substantive commitments in that judgment. First, undergoing the identified aversive episode is bad for its subject in the relevant respect, beyond merely predicting another loss. Second, that burden is a nonexclusive reason-ground: a relevant opportunity to alleviate it can give another capable actor a reason for the patient's sake. The first locates what is at stake. The second explains why it can enter someone else's justified deliberation. Neither is supplied merely by the fact that a learned connection influences a decision.

The strongest present support is comparative. The proposed ground explains the directed object of relief: improving the patient's condition answers why this response matters even if the helper's emotional discomfort disappears. It permits correcting a false inference about a patient's needs without declaring all concern mistaken. It also accounts for the difference between losing access to a consideration and that consideration ceasing to apply. An alternative based exclusively on an observer's reaction must assess those distinctions; describing the reaction's cause does not by itself settle its appropriateness. These considerations give the baseline positive, defeasible support. They do not constitute a deductive proof of universal other-directed reasons, and a richer rival can retain some or all of the same judgments through different grounds.

The conditional foundation can state and defend these commitments as normative premises while explaining their recognition, acquisition and use. It need not wait for every philosophical rival to agree. Conversely, conditional use cannot be reported as derivation of those premises from the minimal causal root. The stronger 慧根 claim additionally requires their necessity across independently adequate inquiry, together with the other foundational commitments. That stronger task is retained.

### What direct acquaintance would establish

The new Dimitrov comparison offers an epistemic route beyond taking a representation at face value. Its proposed constitution of a qualifying judgment by its phenomenal subject matter can secure contact with an actual quality. The source nevertheless leaves both identifying the judgment as qualifying and classifying the quality under the ordinary evaluative concept fallible. This matters to the foundation: reliable contact with a property and a warranted account of its normative significance are connected achievements, not synonyms.

Our reconstruction separates three claims. A token quality is instantiated; it is the adverse quality relevant to the patient-good judgment; and this patient-ground supports the actor-addressed reason. A defended identity can connect the first two without treating value as an arbitrary additional label. The third still needs its interpersonal principle. Merely calling a direct sample bad cannot settle all three, while a successful identity cannot be refuted by varying a symbol as if the identity were absent.

This route does not require the patient to possess the concepts used in its defense. The proposed burden belongs to the subject undergoing it, whether or not the subject can give a theory of introspection. Another actor can acquire warranted knowledge through appropriate evidence without literally undergoing the same episode. Such mediated access has its own reliability conditions; first-person acquaintance is neither a universal diagnostic instrument nor an automatic transfer of the patient's epistemic position to the observer.

### Relational constitution and the scope of a reason

The Brady comparison supplies a serious alternative realization of painfulness: the relevant whole includes sensation and aversion. It also shows why modifying a constituent and modifying a later attitude toward the whole are different changes. The root should not require every plausible account of affect to agree on one constitution before it can assess the particular patient's burden. Nor may it hold only sensory intensity fixed and announce that the entire ground is unchanged.

Two different relations must remain visible. **Whose condition grounds a reason?** and **who has that reason?** are separate questions. A burden can depend on a particular patient without every reason concerning it being confined to that patient. The positive proposal can therefore preserve differentiated centers while extending concern; it need not posit value remaining after every subject and experience disappears. This identifies a possible structure, not an argument that every such structure is valid.

Mack's critique makes the opposing obligation concrete: one can acknowledge another's reason without thereby acquiring that reason, and a self-concept need not be explicit for a relation to oneself to matter. We accept that those are genuine challenges. Our reply to the exclusivity argument is limited: dependence on the patient does not alone decide practical address. Likewise, the lack of an exclusion proof cannot decide it in favor of universal inclusion. Lomasky's reply supplies comparative normative explanation as a positive route, with pro tanto relevance rather than compulsory impartiality as its target. Its success requires more than the prevalence of sympathy or the persistence of a response.

The live premise is thus specific: **a genuinely adverse experience can count for its patient's relief in another capable actor's deliberation without an additional requirement of that actor's antecedent concern**. This concerns the patient's burden and the actor's opportunity, not independence from every patient attitude that might constitute the burden. It leaves the reason's comparative weight, an owed response and intervention permission to their further arguments.

### How a formed evaluation could track this ground

Let V(p,e) express the independently justified patient-good claim about an actual episode, and let L(a,y,e,c) express that action y is an available, appropriately situated relief route in context c. Let B be the defended cross-actor relevance principle. The particular derivation is

\[
V(p,e),\quad L(a,y,e,c),\quad B
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad R(a,p,y,c;e).
\]

R identifies a pro tanto consideration arising from that episode. It is neither an action verdict nor a numerical exchange rate. The assumptions making this a warranted inference must retain the same patient, episode and action meaning. A factually accurate learning process can provide evidence for V's application or L without itself establishing B. It can also correct a mistaken application of an already justified principle.

The useful result is **selective revision of the particular justification**, rather than a rule that learning should always strengthen concern:

| Warranted change | Consequence for this justification |
|---|---|
| A represented injury or danger was absent, while the actual aversive episode remains | Withdraw the defeated world-directed assessment; the independently grounded experiential burden can remain |
| The proposed action would not relieve this episode | Withdraw this means-based support for that action; the burden may still warrant another response |
| The episode has ended, including through a change in a constitutive component | Its current relief application ends; prevention, repair or another episode may supply a different reason |
| Only the helper's distress or desire changes, with the admitted patient-ground and opportunity fixed | Under B, the patient-related consideration survives; intense empathic affect is unnecessary |
| Relevant authority, alternatives or competing claims change | Reassess the permissible response or complete comparison without silently denying the patient's burden |

Mere absence of evidence does not establish that the episode ended; failure to detect an expected sign can be informative under a warranted observation model. The withdrawal of one derivation is not proof that no reason for the same action remains. If removing an aversion changes the patient's whole experience, it is not equivalent to removing the helper's concern. Nor does the mere possibility of changing either component authorize imposing that change on the patient. These distinctions keep correction answerable to its target rather than to the desired sign of a learned response.

This supplies an interface to the current [evaluative-plasticity study](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/EVALUATIVE-PLASTICITY.md). Its inspected numerical result forms and revises an operative connection; changed conductance can suppress an affordable, causally effective service. That demonstrates acquired influence under the supplied law. Which change is warranted depends on the relevant patient-ground and application, not on positive conductance, the occurrence of revision or factual command knowledge alone. Its stocks and teaching events are not measurements of suffering. The accepted conditional applications retain their existing scope; no additional universal implementation gate is introduced.

### What this establishes and what would revise it

The advance is a positive, explicitly grounded account of concern and its correction. A direct experiential identification can strengthen the patient-good premise; a separately defended nonexclusive relevance principle supplies practical address; acquired evidence can make their application accessible and revisable. This is sufficient as an openly conditional explanation of warranted concern. It is not yet a derivation of the entire normative basis from generative organization.

The remaining independent normative commitment is the cross-actor relevance of the identified burden, once its badness is justified. Its defense is assessable without demanding unanimity: a better explanation of the same warranted comparisons, a reason-relevant restriction of scope, or a failure of the patient-good identification would revise different parts. Evidence of actual relief failure changes the local application. Evidence that a training signal merely predicts private advantage changes what has been learned. Neither an indifferent actor nor a helpful controller alone decides the moral argument.

The next connection is **defended experiential badness and nonexclusive relevance → a shared normative basis that survives alternative realizations of affect and learning**. The inquiry must establish which content is common and which exclusions are justified, then connect that basis to the full necessary-root argument. This preserves concern, differentiated patients and open possibilities without treating universal favorable development as already proved.

The previous goal turn was progress; its three substantive hashes and 508-link/276-anchor receipt were revalidated. This round adds four primary readings, philosophical reconstruction and application-dependency analysis. It adds no Lean audit, scientific rerun, empirical result or claim of complete whole-root necessity.

## 30. Experiential occurrence, patient attitudes and whole-life value

6 October 2026. AI-authored continuation of §29, addressing **a warranted patient-ground across alternative accounts of affect and welfare → a shared practical basis → the whole necessary root**. The preceding conditional argument remains usable. This round tests whether its episode-local premise can be carried unchanged into competing accounts. The [primary comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#27-pain-attitudes-and-whole-life-value) adds three works and extends the reading of an existing fourth. Its philosophical cases are not new clinical evidence.

### The positive result and the precise disagreement

A present, still-unwanted aversive episode supplies a defensible local starting point for relief: the patient is undergoing a condition whose cessation matters to that patient, and an effective response ends that condition. The justification proposed here takes the aversion as appropriate to a genuinely adverse experience; a desire's mere existence does not establish that appropriateness. Relief can be intelligible without a private benefit to the helper, a new injury, or a novel kind of experience. With §29's defended interpersonal premise and situated means, this supports a conditional actor-addressed consideration.

This argument has more limited content than saying that phenomenal painfulness alone, under every possible attitude and history, necessarily supplies such a reason. Its positive warrant is the comparison between undergoing and ending the particular burden, supported by the patient's experience and an assessment of relevant alternatives. It remains a normative assessment, not an inference from the causal fact that a subject seeks escape. Another account may explain the same warranted relief through different grounds. Common action, common reason-content and a common sufficient explanation must therefore be assessed separately.

Four distinctions now become decisive:

| Claim | What it establishes if warranted | What it leaves open |
|---|---|---|
| The episode has negative phenomenal character | A claim about the actual experience | Its prudential value and practical address |
| This episode is bad for this patient in a specified respect | A patient-related evaluative ground | Its effect on the value of the whole life |
| The available alternative improves the relevant welfare target | A comparison of actual options under the account | Whether and how it supplies this actor's reason |
| This consideration counts for this actor's relief action | A pro tanto practical reason | Priority, a directed duty and a complete action verdict |

These are not assumed universally independent. A successful identity or constitutive argument could connect them. The point is to identify which connection a proposed explanation establishes, rather than move between them through the word bad.

### Three controlled challenges to episode-local sufficiency

The Cutter–Tye comparison isolates an attitude condition. Ordinary nonveridical pain is compatible with genuine reasons for relief; the decisive disputed case instead retains painfulness while eliminating aversion and unwanted consequences. Their proposed separation is a modal commitment, not a result secured merely by defining different properties. The existing Bain reading now shows why correcting a bodily-error belief and removing an aversion to the experience are different tests; the first does not settle the second. An account on which aversion partly constitutes the painful whole cannot admit its removal as preservation of that whole without argument.

Our resulting test is specific: identify the complete experience and the relevant attitude separately, establish whether their proposed separation is coherent, and then defend the normative verdict on that case. Refusing all empirical relevance to pain reports would be a mistake; treating reports or a philosopher's case description as decisive identification of the complete phenomenal state would also be a mistake. The current record establishes a serious disagreement about sufficiency, not a demonstrated aversion-free painful patient.

The Hawkins comparison introduces a different possibility: appropriate enjoyment can defeat the badness assigned to an unpleasant experience rather than merely provide a compensating benefit. For our reconstruction, accepting pain to achieve another end must be distinguished from positively appreciating the very experience. A volunteered ordeal, persistence through training, or a helper's enjoyment does not establish the latter. Whether the entire experiential whole changes, and whether the relevant positive attitude can have this value-altering role, must be assessed. We need not accept this account to recognize that an always-bad premise owes it a substantive reply. Its allowance for bad experience without sophisticated attitudes also prevents reducing the alternatives to a choice between articulate preference and no patient-ground.

The Bramble comparison challenges a different dependency: the value bearer can be the whole life's containing a qualitative kind, rather than independently valuable episode-tokens. Repetition may then leave that target unchanged. This is not merely a low weight assigned to the later token, nor a claim that the first token is intrinsically bad while a duplicate is intrinsically neutral. It is a proposed nonadditive account, supported philosophically rather than established by an empirical observation of unbotheredness.

### A concrete consequence of changing the value bearer

For an original ordinal illustration, let H0, H1 and H2 be complete alternative lives containing zero, one and two otherwise controlled occurrences of one pain-kind. An account can consistently rank them

\[
H_0\succ H_1\sim H_2.
\]

These are rankings of lives, not independently assigned token values. No numerical addition is assumed. Preventing a second occurrence after the first would then leave this life-level value unchanged, even though a life without either occurrence would be better. There is no contradiction in the combination. A strict ranking H0 ≻ H1 ≻ H2 needs an additional monotonicity commitment; qualitative similarity alone does not supply it.

Let z(H) record only the features that a proposed welfare account regards as value-bearing. If an intervention leaves z unchanged and the account's welfare ordering depends entirely on z, it cannot count that intervention as a strict improvement of this target. This is an elementary reuse of the [representation criterion](HUIGEN-GENERATIVE-CLOSURE.md#h13-the-operational-invariant-is-a-preserved-question), not a new formal result or evidence that the account is correct. In an actual application, the relevant alternatives must be feasible prospective continuations; the illustration does not imply an ability to erase past experience.

The consequence is bounded: a welfare-mediated argument requiring that improvement loses a premise. It does not follow that there is no independently grounded episode-related reason, promise, protective duty or claim against imposition. Conversely, invoking such a reason as a reply must defend its ground. One cannot silently change V from whole-life value to local reason and report that the original derivation survived.

Our occurrence-sensitive defense challenges the proposed restriction at its strongest point. If the patient still undergoes an appropriately unwanted aversive condition, why should the absence of a new qualitative kind remove the significance of ending that condition? The local account explains this judgment by the actual occurrence and its cessation; it does not need to calculate a sum of episode values. The holistic rival must explain why this present-relief judgment is misleading or supported elsewhere. The local account, in turn, owes an explanation of its apparent conflict with the holistic comparison. This is comparative normative argument, not a deductive refutation. The mundane familiarity of a repeated ache cannot settle every severe, unwanted case, and adding severity by stipulation cannot itself prove the favored verdict.

### The shared interface must retain the comparison

Section 29's V(p,e), L(a,y,e,c), B ⇒ R(a,p,y,c;e) remains a valid openly conditional proposal when V concerns the identified episode and L effects the relevant relief. The extension to another account must retain more than p and the name of e. It must preserve the value-bearing target, the comparison used to assess it, the action's effect on that target, and the circumstances that defeat the ground or its application.

For a welfare-mediated route, make this explicit by naming the target t and the context c: J(p,t,c) is the warranted patient-good assessment, Δ(y,t,c) says that the feasible action makes the improvement required by this assessment, and B supplies its practical relevance to actor a. Their justified composition supports a reason of the stated kind. Here J, Δ and B are substantive commitments, not a definition of scientific adequacy. An episode-based account and a life-based account can sometimes agree on this complete inference; mere agreement that y ends e is insufficient to show that they do.

An interpretation which preserves a conclusion through a different justification can establish common content but not the necessity of our particular explanatory root. This retains the earlier whole-theory obligation: one faithful joint interpretation must carry the root and its sound consequences. Finding a small overlapping set of ordinary relief cases is useful for application; it is not a replacement for establishing the entire proposed foundation. No unanimity requirement is introduced. Rivals matter through the force of their arguments and countercases, not through their mere existence.

### Connection to formed evaluation and selective revision

The current [plasticity account](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/EVALUATIVE-PLASTICITY.md) now records numerical and formal acceptance of a finite formation/change/use construction. This chat reads that as inherited evidence at its declared causal scope; it does not rerun its checks. A link that predicts episode relief cannot be redescribed as tracking lifetime improvement or another actor's obligation without the corresponding interpretation and argument. Its supplied calibration, native evaluation and teaching-to-service relation remain visible.

Once a particular ground and practical address are independently warranted, a change only in the learner's association need not defeat them. Warranted revision instead changes the premise actually affected: the patient's experiential whole, a relevant attitude, the welfare comparison, the means, the actor's responsibility or the ground's current applicability. Genuine intrinsic enjoyment may require a different patient assessment on a defended account; mere learned indifference in the actor does not have that same role. A favorable coefficient likewise cannot make an ended or inapplicable burden recur.

A separate role-bound repair argument can use an actual attributable loss and a justified responsibility to restore it. Such a premise could support service even where a generic experiential argument is unsettled. The existence of a reason to repair a loss does not justify causing that loss to create a learning opportunity. Nor does this conditional illustration identify the experiment's recipient stock with welfare, establish who owes repair, or count as a new implementation. The accepted finite construction retains its scope.

### Result, revision conditions and next connection

This round strengthens the positive conditional foundation by identifying the exact preservation task: the same patient, actual condition, value bearer, alternative comparison and practical address. It also isolates a substantive candidate commitment, **occurrence-sensitive experiential relevance**, which can be defended without assuming additive lifetime welfare. That commitment is not yet proved necessary across all adequate inquiry.

A justified aversion-free countercase would restrict pure phenomenal sufficiency. A justified positive-attitude defeater would require the patient's relevant attitude in the scope conditions. A successful whole-life account could defeat a particular marginal-improvement argument while leaving other grounds open. A defense showing that each new aversive occurrence retains its own practical relevance would support the local route without automatically establishing its interpersonal extension. These are different possible revisions, not generic demands for more evidence.

The next connection is **occurrence-sensitive patient significance → a warranted actor-addressed ground whose use survives changes in the learner's association**. The study must compare genuine alteration or fulfillment of that ground with changes only in acquired influence, and justify the actor's scope. This connects the experiential argument to formed evaluation without substituting an operational success measure for the authority being explained. The full necessary root and its indefinitely many valid consequences remain the objective.

The previous goal turn was progress; its three substantive hashes and 517-link/282-anchor receipt were revalidated. This round adds primary close reading and philosophical argument. It adds no scientific run, empirical result, Lean audit, new controller or claim of whole-foundation completion.

## 31. Objective inquiry, practical access and generative growth

6 October 2026. AI-authored continuation of §§29–30. The named connection is **faithful knowledge of a patient-ground → a reason addressed to the acting process → sound growth of the whole generative account**. The preceding turn made progress: its canonical, audit and source hashes were revalidated against the 523-link/287-anchor receipt. This round examines the interpersonal step and whether acquired capacities can change practical applicability without making endorsement the source of truth. The [new primary comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#28-objective-understanding-practical-access-and-new-reasons) records exact access and reading limits.

### A positive application is now established conditionally

The independently reviewed [evaluative-grounding argument](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/EVALUATIVE-GROUNDING.md) connects an actual formed disposition to a warranted role-bound repair. Its ground concerns the later operation's own uncompensated loss, with independently defended entitlement, responsibility, adequate remedy and priority. A justified entrusted role is needed when an operator's responsibility is implemented by a learner. The physical schedule does not itself establish authorship or delegation.

This is substantive positive progress in the common foundation. The acquired connection makes a causal difference to the response that the independent argument recommends. Under the same applicable repair interpretation, its matched replacement can suppress an affordable effective remedy and count as mistaken revision. The conditionally warranted response is not simply read off a favorable output, and the later loss is not an old fulfilled service claim relabeled pending. This chat inherits the [accepted assessment](EVALUATIVE-GROUNDING-ASSESSMENT.md) and [review](EVALUATIVE-GROUNDING-REVIEW.md); it adds no compiler run, simulation or independent empirical verification.

The broader question is now sharper. A justified particular role gives one connection between patient and actor. Mere capability to help is a different proposed connection. Showing that a learner can implement a responsible institution's repair does not establish that every unrelated actor acquires the same duty, or that only responsible actors can have reasons to help. Each extension needs its own ground.

### Accurate attribution and practical address
