A faithful account of a reason must preserve its addressee. If the evidence establishes R(operator,p,y), an observer can accurately know that relation without its becoming R(observer,p,y). This is not a skeptical reinterpretation that denies all moral facts. It preserves the attributed reason as true while asking what additional relation, if any, connects it to the observer's action. The earlier symmetry and transport results already distinguish coherent relabeling from enlarging practical scope.

The newly inspected Nagel1986 selection makes the positive route and its limit explicit: objective acknowledgment of relative reasons need not appropriate them, while sensory value is offered as a ground for impersonal concern through a further substantive judgment. The current task therefore cannot be closed merely by calling the patient condition objective. We must justify why its particular significance has the claimed practical address.

McDowell provides a constructive alternative to a choice between detached fact and extra desire. Acquired sensitivity can form part of understanding a situation as reason-giving. That proposal supports a possible account of learning concern: the learner comes to understand something practically significant, rather than adding a reward to an unchanged neutral description. But it also constrains the necessity argument. Ordinary linguistic or descriptive competence does not automatically include that richer understanding, and nonhypothetical authority need not guarantee recognition by every otherwise rational person.

Our reconstruction distinguishes four achievements: identifying the patient's condition, attributing a reason correctly, understanding its practical significance, and acting in accordance with it. They may be constitutively connected under a defended account; they cannot be stipulated identical to establish that account. In particular, a controller that preserves factual command knowledge while changing choice does not thereby demonstrate a person who possesses complete virtuous understanding and is unmoved. Conversely, a person who lacks such understanding is not by that fact alone shown to be incapable of accurate scientific inquiry.

The positive inquiry-to-concern argument must consequently establish both **what the relevant understanding includes** and **why adequate inquiry of the claimed scope requires it**. This is a specific obligation concerning practical content, rather than a demand for universal persuasion. A true claim does not lose its authority because some competent people reject it. Yet the absence of universal agreement cannot be used to assume the very necessary connection being investigated.

### Actor capacity is different from patient standing

The new Nagel2023 comparison introduces a substantive condition of practical address: an in-principle path to understanding a reason, distinguished from actually accepting it. Its account also allows patient value without a current population of rational reason-holders. This matters here because it separates two questions often merged by an undifferentiated appeal to rationality.

A patient need not formulate a reason to undergo a burden. A possible reason-holder may need a relevant capacity to understand or respond; exactly which capacity is necessary is disputed. Excluding a patient because it cannot deliberate does not follow from restricting the addressee of a deliberative reason. Nor does a learner's failure to act show that it lacked every possible route to understanding. An unwilling, inattentive or misinformed actor differs from one incapable of the relevant understanding.

We treat accessibility as a candidate normative condition, not an established universal result. The move from ability to perform an action to ability to understand its reason needs argument; those are different abilities. Liability, blame, a directed claim, and a reason for action can also have different conditions. A patient may retain a claim addressed to an institution even where one component implementing its response is not itself a deliberating moral agent. The accepted grounding application does not require each component to entertain the complete justification.

This gives a useful correction to a too-simple rule that a learner's change can never matter to a reason. A mere change in association does not establish a relevant new capacity, but genuine changes in capacity, opportunity or an entrusted role can change practical applicability under a justified principle. The patient's underlying burden can remain throughout. Which change occurred is an identification and justification question, not something settled by the sign of the learned connection.

### Three kinds of growth under a possible necessary root

The whole 慧根 objective concerns an indefinitely developing body of justified understanding. It need not assert that every actor has exactly the same unconditional reasons throughout every history. A necessary conditional principle can have changing instances as its actual conditions develop.

For an original schematic reconstruction, let a candidate justified basis B contain

\[
G(p,e,t)\land C(a,t)\land L(a,y,e,t)
\;\Longrightarrow\;R(a,p,y,e,t).
\]

G is the independently warranted patient-ground; C is the relevant, justified actor condition; L identifies an available response under its necessary application conditions. The time argument preserves the actual circumstance. This display specifies neither the truth of B nor whether C should be responsibility, a general understanding-capacity, or another relation. Its point is to track three genuinely different developments:

| Development | What changes | What supports the new conclusion |
|---|---|---|
| Discovery | Evidence or recognition of an already applicable ground and actor relation | The same principle and conditions, newly warranted for the learner |
| New applicability | A relevant capacity, role or opportunity actually forms | The same principle with a newly satisfied condition |
| Revision of the principle | The proposed ground-to-reason connection is corrected or extended | An additional justification of that revised connection |

If G, C and L become jointly established, the existing conditional yields R for that situation. Their earlier non-establishment did not prove ¬R; even a proved absence of one antecedent would defeat only this sufficient route unless that condition were also justified as necessary. In particular, mere lack of evidence must not be confused with lack of access or absence of a ground.

This is sound conditional composition, not a new proof that learning produces moral truth. It is compatible with the [finite-basis and whole-interpretation result](HUIGEN-GENERATIVE-CLOSURE.md#h10-whole-interpretation-and-finite-basis-reduction): a common root can generate context-sensitive conclusions while local facts retain their status as supplied premises. An unchanging principle can govern the formation of new normative relations. Whether it actually governs them requires substantive defense.

### What historical novelty would and would not refute

The inspected Nagel2023 argument goes beyond simple factual change: it proposes that some new normative access depends on historical development. We need not settle that proposal here to identify the challenge it poses. A new conclusion may involve a previously applicable truth, a newly applicable relation, or a genuinely revised normative basis. Treating every such case as discovery of an already explicit timeless verdict would conceal the difference.

Plurality and historical dependence alone do not refute a finite necessary root. A finite basis can contain several irreducible principles, and a conditional can address institutions or capacities that do not yet exist. Conversely, writing a short rule that endorses whatever future wisdom endorses supplies no independent ground or validity-preserving method. It names the unresolved output rather than deriving it. An informative generator must explain why the proposed development is warranted and which conditions support it.

Nor does a difficulty articulating a complete moral code prove that there is no finite explanatory foundation. The earlier closure argument is semantic; it does not claim a finite agent can enumerate, recognize or execute every consequence. A learned practical capacity may exceed what its possessor can state as rules. But explaining that capacity's formation is still different from proving the validity and complete coverage of all its judgments.

If revision introduces a normative premise not derived from B or independently warranted local context, the resulting conclusion is not yet shown to belong to B's justified closure. A successful metaprinciple for revision could extend the account; that metaprinciple would itself need a justified scope and interpretation. This preserves the strength of Ren's proposal without allowing the phrase generative root to conceal an unlimited source of new assumptions.

### Warranted use and the remaining necessity argument

The accepted repair case now provides a concrete two-stage connection. First establish the actual consideration, patient, responsible party and history. Then justify its application to the acting process through responsibility, role or another defended relation, with means, authority and comparison preserved. The existing acquired disposition demonstrably mediates the selected action under that interpretation. This is already conditionally closed for the stated case; it is not a new acceptance gate.

Its matched association change need not change entitlement, responsibility or priority, so it can fail the unchanged reason. That contrast supports a distinction between causal learning and warranted revision. It does not settle whether an ideally developed understanding would necessarily motivate, or whether unrelated actors possess the same ground. Those claims require the richer practical and interpersonal argument.

This round therefore advances the whole-root programme in two ways. It identifies practical address as a content-preservation question with a justified actor relation, and it permits genuinely developing reason-instances under stable principles. It rejects neither objective value nor the possibility of a necessary finite root. What it does rule out is completing that root by moving unnoticed from accurately attributing another's reason to possessing it oneself, or from newly acquired approval to justified new principle.

The next central connection is **patient significance plus the relevant actor capacity → a justified nonexclusive practical ground**, tested against a view that accepts objective understanding while retaining agent-relative address. An argument showing that the patient-ground supplies such a reason independently of role would enlarge the conditional repair result. A warranted capacity restriction would narrow the addressee class without erasing patient standing. A demonstrated need for a further normative principle would revise the candidate basis rather than be concealed as a consequence of factual inquiry.

No scientific run, empirical finding, new Lean audit or controller is added. The new primary readings, original reconstruction and integration are progress toward the full necessary foundation, whose substantive necessity and complete normative coverage remain open.

## 32. Phenomenal identity, practical scope and the necessary root

6 October 2026. AI-authored continuation of §31. The named connection is **intrinsic experiential badness → a reason for an unrelated capable actor → a jointly necessary generative basis**. The preceding turn was progress; root revalidated its three substantive hashes and the 532-link/292-anchor receipt. This round examines a substantive identity proposal rather than assuming that ethical properties must be detachable from experiential ones. The [primary dossier](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#29-phenomenal-identity-and-the-scope-of-intrinsic-value) records one complete author précis and a bounded selection from a second work.

The common foundation has meanwhile reached [root acceptance of the conditional candidate](FOUNDATION-ROOT-ACCEPTANCE.md) under its original five unchanged conditions. Root read that assessment in full, including its positive construction, interpretation, composition, regime and evidence grounds. This branch inherits that scope; it does not repeat its scientific verification. The stronger necessary-root claim pursued here remains a separate research objective. Finding that an additional necessity argument is needed does not reopen the accepted conditional construction or add a new foundation-completion gate.

### An identity can supply substantive normative content

Rawlette proposes that valenced phenomenal qualities supply the content of intrinsic-value concepts. Her interpersonal case separately invokes the experienced claim for help, the causal relevance of self and others to relief, and a challenge to privileging personal continuity. The proposal therefore deserves evaluation as an account of what value is and whom it concerns, rather than dismissal as a freely replaceable label. [Author précis](https://utilitarianism.net/guest-essays/precis-of-the-feeling-of-value/).

Our original reconstruction separates a property identity from its practical role. Let Q identify a particular negative experiential quality in an actual episode e of patient p. Let D be the precisely specified candidate intrinsic disvalue property. Let C identify a relevant actor capacity, and L an available intervention's effect on that same episode under its stated application conditions. The proposed bridge is

\[
Q=D,\qquad
D(p,e)\land C(a,c)\land L(a,y,p,e,c)
\Longrightarrow R(a,p,y,c;e).
\]

The identity notation concerns properties, not an equation between their numerical measurements. R is a contributory reason for the stated intervention, not an all-things-considered instruction, permission to act, or a directed claim against the actor. The circumstances c must retain the relevant defeaters and application conditions. This is a candidate substantive basis, not a theorem establishing its truth.

If the identity and role are independently warranted, identification of Q and the actual relief relation yields a new reason-instance. The analyst need not install a moral reward or assume the final R-instance as a local observation. This is a positive route to reason-generating content within a finite basis. It also changes the force of the earlier [language-relative non-entailment result](#28-suffering-and-the-scope-of-the-missing-normative-bridge): arbitrary reassignments of R may satisfy a norm-free transition language while failing this defended interpretation. They would no longer be countermodels to the enriched theory. An identity argument must be assessed on its merits before declaring those reassignments genuinely admissible.

Three different contents must nevertheless remain visible. A property can concern what is bad for its bearer, what is intrinsically bad, or what gives every eligible actor a reason to oppose its occurrence. These may be connected by a successful account; writing one expression for all three does not establish their identity. In particular, intrinsic grounding concerns what the value depends on, while practical address concerns whose reason it is.

A conceptual test also has limited reach. Excluding a description that makes an unpleasant experience intrinsically good in every respect is weaker than proving that this quality exhausts all intrinsic badness, that no other thing has intrinsic value, or that its action relevance has universal actor scope. Rejecting the extreme description can support a local evaluative constraint. The stronger identity, exclusivity and scope conclusions require their own arguments. Conversely, failure of exclusivity would not by itself defeat a sufficient ground of concern in the identified episode.

### Three tests of nonexclusive practical significance

The following are our assessments of the three interpersonal routes, not claims that the précis has empirically established universal moral perception.

**The felt claim for help.** A seeming that another ought to help offers positive normative evidence. It should neither be treated as normatively empty merely because it is experiential nor declared infallible because it is immediate. Its evidential force depends on what the subject actually apprehends and how that judgment survives correction. Identifying an adverse quality and identifying a claim addressed to another are distinguishable tasks even if one experience presents both. Agreement about the first can coexist with disagreement about the second. Independent warrant for the full practical content would advance the bridge substantially; a learned help-expectation alone would not.

**Preservation when the effective operator changes.** In the proposed comparison, the same patient's adverse episode is relieved through either of two available causal routes. The helper's reproduction of the patient's distress is unnecessary to identify whose condition improves. This preservation supplies a strong positive reason to investigate why the very same disvalue would cease to matter when another person can alleviate it. But effective causal control and normative address are different relations. A view restricting reasons by actor–patient relation must explain its restriction; an unrestricted account must justify why changing that relation does not eliminate the ground's practical force. Absence of an explicitly represented self in a quality does not itself settle whether a normative relation is present.

**Continuity and the alleged privilege of self.** Denying a separate enduring substance does not remove every fact about memory, agency, dependence or relationships. The earlier branching and succession studies already preserve this distinction. A relation can remain real and reason-relevant in a process account. The strongest positive route therefore asks what could justify making the identical kind of patient disvalue exclusively relevant to its own temporally connected actor. A successful argument against that exclusion could establish broader relevance. It need not abolish special responsibilities or prove equal weight for every actor in every circumstance. Mere metaphysical reduction does not establish ethical irrelevance.

These tests leave a defensible intermediate target: **the patient's identified disvalue gives an unrelated eligible actor some reason for effective relief, even where it supplies no reciprocal benefit**. That target is narrower than hedonistic exclusivity, aggregate maximization, or universal blame. It can coexist with plural goods and differentiated centers. Its sufficient grounds still require defense; isolating it prevents stronger disputed conclusions from being smuggled into a baseline concern principle.

### Intrinsic value need not settle the structure of action reasons

The bounded Wedgwood comparison distinguishes intrinsic value, attitudes fitting for anyone, and action reasons sensitive to agential involvement; the latter can remain nonconsequentialist. It supports examining the action-role premise explicitly. It does not establish that an unrelated actor has no baseline reason. [Selected primary passages, especially 325 and 337](https://philarchive.org/archive/WEDAPT).

Our inference is methodological and substantive. If Q is identified with a value property whose justified content already includes universal fittingness of a response, that content cannot be omitted when testing a rival interpretation. Yet fitting concern and a reason for a particular intervention remain different claims unless their connection is established. Available means, the kind of participation, permission and competing reasons can matter. A theory can recognize everyone's suffering while rejecting a rule that simply selects the largest aggregate improvement.

This also gives a more exact counterexample requirement. A serious rival must preserve the very property and practical role actually justified, not merely preserve numerical behavior while changing value meanings. Conversely, a proposed identity cannot protect itself by defining every dissenting interpretation as inadequate before its disputed role has been vindicated. The task is to assess the grounds of that role, not to count agreement or to rely on formal consistency alone.

### A small separation of baseline significance and aggregation

For an original hand illustration, stipulate two fixed patients, a common cardinal scale of remaining burden, and two feasible outcomes:

\[
A=(0,10),\qquad B=(6,6).
\]

A rule minimizing total burden prefers A, since 10 is less than 12. A rule lexicographically minimizing the ordered pair (maximum burden, minimum burden) prefers B, since its maximum is 6 rather than 10. Both rules are invariant under relabeling the patients. Both strictly prefer an outcome where either patient's burden decreases and the other's remains unchanged: under the second rule the maximum cannot rise, and if it stays unchanged the minimum falls.

Thus even these stronger stipulated resources—commensurability, patient anonymity and strict improvement for a single patient with all else fixed—do not determine a unique verdict when their burdens conflict. The illustration does not establish that either rule is ethically valid, independently establish interpersonal measurement, or refute the fuller case for an aggregate rule. It isolates an additional explanatory obligation. A universal baseline concern principle can be sound without yielding one complete ordering of every conflict.

This is important for the theory's preservation of plural centers. Deriving some practical significance for each center would be a substantial achievement. Deriving the priority structure among their claims is further work. No result here reduces a patient to an interchangeable quantity or licenses using another's body merely because the sum improves. Those would require separate arguments about authority and permissible action.

### What this would establish for 慧根

A successful identity-plus-role argument could enrich the generative root with an independently defended source of normative content. Actual identification, acquired understanding and effective means could then generate new justified applications, as §31 describes. Its truth would not depend on whether the learner happens to reinforce the helpful response. The causal foundation explains formation, retention and correction of influence; the normative account explains when that influence is faithful to a reason.

This does not require every realization of inquiry to contain suffering. A necessary conditional could govern such episodes wherever they occur. Nor does it require reducing all value to one quality: a finite basis may include several irreducible grounds. The [whole-interpretation argument](HUIGEN-GENERATIVE-CLOSURE.md#h10-whole-interpretation-and-finite-basis-reduction) still requires that the basis hold jointly with faithful meaning and cover the claims actually retained. Establishing one baseline reason is progress toward that obligation, not proof that the entire current formulation is indispensable.

The next decisive connection is **independently identified phenomenal disvalue → justified nonexclusive practical relevance**. The current routes make the evidence and burden concrete: assess whether the helping claim belongs to warranted recognition of the quality, whether a relational restriction explains anything normatively relevant in the matched relief case, and whether a proposed identity includes precisely the practical role defended. A sound identity with that role would rule out the corresponding free reason-reassignment. A justified actor restriction, a defeater of the quality's sufficient disvalue, or a better-supported plural account would change different parts of the candidate basis. Aggregate priority and N3 answerability must retain their separate grounds.

The conditional candidate's completion remains accepted. This round adds primary reading, original conceptual analysis and one hand-checked comparison; it adds no empirical result, formal declaration, simulation, controller or construction gate. It advances the stronger necessity programme without treating the lack of a whole-root proof as a refutation of the candidate.

## 33. Evolutionary genealogy and the justification of concern

6 October 2026. AI-authored continuation of §32. The named connection is **an identified patient-ground → warranted nonexclusive concern → a necessary root capable of correcting its own acquired judgments**. The preceding turn was progress: root revalidated its canonical, audit and source hashes against the 540-link/296-anchor receipt. The new [primary comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#30-evolutionary-genealogy-and-warranted-concern) tests whether an account of how judgments arose can justify preferring impartial concern over an exclusive focus on oneself.

The [conditional foundation remains accepted](FOUNDATION-ROOT-ACCEPTANCE.md) under its original five criteria. This study investigates a stronger justificatory and necessity claim. It does not impose a new construction requirement, rerun the accepted mechanisms or treat the absence of a unique philosophical foundation as their failure.

### The strongest affirmative argument includes positive warrant

De Lazari-Radek and Singer defend substantive benevolence through reflection, independent agreement and resistance to explanations that disconnect judgment from truth. They propose that an evolved general reasoning capacity can reach truths beyond its original adaptive uses. Selective debunking then weakens a conflicting egoist intuition. They expressly allow coherent egoism, reject deriving benevolence by generalization alone, and characterize their affirmative account as a reasonable hypothesis. [2012 primary article, §§III–V](https://www.journals.uchicago.edu/doi/10.1086/667837).

This supplies a serious route for our programme. The origins of a capacity need not exhaust what it can validly discover. A generative process can produce resources for examining its inherited preferences, and its subsequent conclusions need not be valuable merely because they helped its ancestors survive. Our accepted closure result already distinguishes the historical production of a reasoning apparatus from the validity of what follows under its justified premises and sound rules.

But this possibility does not identify a particular moral judgment as a deliverance of reliable reason. The disputed proposition and the grounds supporting it must still be inspected. Neither the absence of direct adaptive benefit nor the presence of abstract reasoning certifies it. Sound inference can extend a mistaken premise, and a harmful reproductive consequence can accompany either insight or error. The affirmative route is strongest when it supplies independent reasons to accept the patient-ground and practical principle, then explains how the actual learner can apprehend them.

### Preserve the good that the interpersonal principle is meant to extend

Kahane's direct reply challenges the proposed asymmetry: the debunking strategy must retain warranted beliefs about patient good if impartiality is to guide action. Defenses appealing to reason or substantive convictions may also be available to partialists. His distinctions among self-interest, priority and exclusive egoism sharpen the target. [2014 author manuscript, §§1–3 and notes9,16,35](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC3975828/).

Our conclusion is narrower than either dismissing all genealogy or accepting the reply as a proof that every ethical belief has equal standing. The same standard must examine both the ground being extended and the proposed restriction. A principle that tells an actor to give proper weight to every patient's good supplies no identified reason-instance when the account has defeated all of its own evidence about what is good. Conversely, explaining the formation of a protective response does not, without further premises, defeat its present justification.

This makes the two sides of the current root inseparable. We need warrant for the actual episode's disvalue and for its relevance beyond the patient. An explanation that protects the latter by discrediting every evolved evaluative judgment may destroy the former's only acknowledged support. A successful selective argument must identify the particular evidential dependence it undercuts and explain why the surviving patient-ground has adequate support. Merely calling that support introspection or reason is insufficient; neither label names an infallible process.

### Three different claims about self

For an original clarification, distinguish:

| Claim | Content | Relation to baseline concern |
|---|---|---|
| S | My own adverse condition gives me some reason to respond | Compatible with reasons concerning other patients |
| X | Only my own adverse condition can give me such a reason | Excludes the proposed other-patient baseline |
| P | My own or a related person's claim has priority in a specified conflict | Needs a conflict-specific ground; does not necessarily exclude others |

These are schematic contents, not claims that the same human judgment always has one of these exact forms. A causal explanation of self-preferring behavior does not identify which proposition the actor believes or which justificatory route supports it. Behavior can reflect habit, mistaken means knowledge, an exclusion principle, a defensible special responsibility or a mixture of these. Self-preserving behavior, felt salience of one’s pain, a prudential belief and an explicit denial of all other-patient reasons are different outputs. An explanation of the first two does not yet identify the production or reliability of the last. The ground and the actually operative mechanism have to be identified separately.

The minimal interpersonal task concerns X, together with positive support for an actor's reason concerning another patient. Defeating X does not by itself prove that support: suspension of judgment remains possible. Yet establishing a reason for another patient does not require deleting S. Nor must every P be rejected. Preserving the subject's own good and separately justified special relationships is consistent with concern for strangers. The theory can seek nonexclusive concern while keeping differentiated centers and justified plurality intact.

### Revision should follow justificatory dependence

A causal history and a justification answer different questions. Two beliefs may share an evolutionary ancestor or learning architecture yet have different current evidential support. A conclusion initially accepted for a poor reason can later acquire an adequate justification. Conversely, a newly articulated argument can still depend on the very assumption whose authority has been challenged. Mere novelty, reflective language or cultural distance does not establish independence.

Our positive conditional route therefore has four parts. First, identify the proposition and the evidence or inference actually offered for it. Second, substantiate the challenge to that route: show why the relevant process does not support confidence in this claim, rather than merely observing that the process has causes. Third, assess other available grounds under the same standards, including their substantive premises and possible shared error. Fourth, revise the conclusions that actually depend on the defeated warrant, retaining those with adequate independent support. All four require argument or evidence appropriate to their subject matter.

Here independence concerns the defeated justification, not total freedom from shared causal ancestry. Demanding the latter would disqualify the evolved capacities used to criticize evolution itself. An ordinary causal account can be compatible with truth-sensitive cognition; an adaptive advantage alone does not establish that sensitivity. Which description fits a particular route is a further empirical and epistemic question. This study supplies no newly validated evolutionary history or general reliability estimate.

A small dependency illustration makes the affirmative contribution precise. Suppose an agent formerly supported a claim q only through an intuition i. Evidence now undercuts i's evidential authority. A separate argument a, whose premises and inferences are adequately supported without relying on i's disputed authority, may still warrant q. If a merely reformulates i or relies on a rule established from the same defeated support, it does not repair that loss. Shared biological ancestry by itself decides neither case. The illustration tracks justification; it does not infer q's falsity from lost support, or truth from the existence of a surviving argument label.

This is continuous with the accepted [application and correction account](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/EVALUATIVE-APPLICATION.md): withdraw a defeated dependence while preserving independent grounds and the actual patient. The present extension concerns epistemic support for the normative premise itself. It does not claim that the finite learner implements this philosophical assessment, recognizes responsibility as responsibility, or autonomously discovers ethical validity.

### What recovered warrant can establish

Let W abbreviate warranted acceptance under the stated evidence and epistemic standards. Let G be an actual patient-ground, B a proposed nonexclusive practical principle and L the relevant means and application conditions. With a warranted, appropriately reliable method of combining these premises, the positive target is

\[
W(G),\;W(B),\;W(L)
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad W(R(a,p,y;c)).
\]

This display tracks conditional epistemic support; it does not assert that warrant is infallible or automatically preserved by every form of aggregation. The combination must respect uncertainty, defeaters, the actor and the same patient. For a deductive application, its warranted premises and inference supply the corresponding reasoned acceptance. A historical explanation can strengthen or weaken support for one premise without changing all the others.

In particular, reduced warrant for an exclusion claim does not constitute W(B), and B alone does not supply W(G). An independently supported B may become better justified relative to a rival when a genuine defeater removes that rival's competing evidential support. That is positive comparative progress when B's own grounds remain adequate. It is stronger than mere non-refutation, while weaker than establishing that B is necessary under every faithful interpretation of adequate inquiry.

The recovered-warrant route can be stated more sharply. Suppose B has adequate positive support for nonexclusive practical relevance, an actual exclusion judgment E challenges it, justified evidence defeats E’s only sufficient current support, and the challenge neither defeats B’s support nor leaves another undefeated objection. Then the former challenge no longer blocks B’s warrant. If the positive support establishes only badness for the patient, however, the interpersonal conclusion remains unsupported by this route. These conditions state which evidential change does the work; they do not certify that every real case satisfies them.

A relative counterinterpretation fixes the scope. An account can retain baseline reasons concerning everyone, abandon an unsupported exclusion and preserve an independently justified promise or role priority. It satisfies the narrower recovery argument while denying that all reasons must be impartial or benevolence always overrides. This does not establish the complete mixed account as true; it shows what the stated premises leave open. The stronger conclusion would require a further argument about those retained priorities.

Care is also needed with disagreement and convergence. Agreement is useful evidence only to the extent that its epistemic role is justified; numerous judgments copied from one source are not automatically many independent confirmations. Shared ancestry likewise does not show that all convergent reasoning is one error. Evaluating this requires the actual routes and the relevant alternatives. Neither disagreement nor agreement can substitute for that assessment.

The patient's condition itself must remain distinct from the learner's confidence in its value. Discovering that one route to a belief was unreliable changes what the learner may responsibly claim. It does not, on its own, end the patient's suffering or settle that the experience lacks value. An effective intervention's causal effect can remain the same while the justification for choosing it is being reassessed. Other independently warranted responsibilities may survive throughout.

### Consequences for the whole generative root

The result advances the proposed 慧根 in a concrete way. A generative explanation can include the origins of evaluative capacities, the causes of their errors, and justified selection among their later methods. But adaptive retention, acquisition and endorsement do not certify the substantive starting points. Each must retain its actual evidential and normative status in the generated account. The theory should be able to explain why a produced judgment is mistaken without declaring every product of its producer mistaken.

The earlier necessity-transfer result remains conditional on a justified necessary basis and sound generation. This round neither proves that basis by evolutionary genealogy nor rules out its existence. It identifies a route through warranted correction and a specific failure mode: removing an opponent's intuitive support while silently retaining equally challenged support for one's own patient-ground. The remedy is a discriminating account of current justification, not an assumption that no discrimination is possible.

The next central connection is **reliable access to experiential significance → warranted practical relevance beyond the subject**. The new comparison places a concrete burden on the access account: explain why the identified experiential route supports the evaluative judgment, rather than only the occurrence of the sensation, and assess its relation to the mechanism that produced the judgment. The practical-scope argument then has to preserve that ground without importing universal concern into its definition. A substantiated asymmetry of warrant would strengthen the account; a shared undefeated error, a misidentified experiential property or an independently warranted restriction would revise it. Baseline concern, aggregate priority, directed answerability and whole-root necessity remain distinct tasks.

No new scientific run, empirical result, formal declaration or operating requirement is added. The conditional foundation's acceptance is preserved. This is progress toward the stronger full objective through primary comparison, a more precise exclusion target and an account of correction that can retain justified patient significance.

## 34. Experiential reliability and the necessity of generated claims

6 October 2026. AI-authored continuation of §33. The named connection is **reliable experiential access → warranted patient significance → nonexclusive practical relevance → the whole necessary root**. The preceding goal turn was progress: the 551-link/300-anchor receipt retained 55 historical rows, and all 28 recorded artifact hashes matched actual bytes before this round's edits. The [primary comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#31-experiential-access-and-transferable-evaluative-reliability) investigates an affirmative inductive route and a precise challenge about what experience it concerns. The [conditional foundation remains accepted](FOUNDATION-ROOT-ACCEPTANCE.md); the stronger necessity investigation adds no completion gate.

### A positive route through reliability

Sinhababu proposes identifying belief-forming processes, assessing their reliability and using that evidence to update moral credence. His introspection example requires both psychological unity and predictability of correctness across domains. He acknowledges hidden parameters and incorporates prior evidence, logical complexity and multiple sources. The hedonist illustration is a hypothesis requiring empirical support. This is an inductive epistemology, not a deduction from definitions. [Primary article, §§1–3, with the reading limits recorded in the dossier](https://philpapers.org/archive/NEITRR.pdf).

This route deserves a substantive test. It could warrant an evaluative judgment without first proving a phenomenal/value identity or postulating a separate faculty that detects moral properties. Conversely, identifying one psychological mechanism does not alone establish the reliability of every judgment it produces. A route used successfully for one kind of content may depend on inputs or conditions absent from another. The question is whether the proposed process classification supports the particular transfer, not whether the target happens to carry the label moral.

Our reconstruction has three obligations. Identify the actual process and the evaluative judgment attributed to it. Defend why evidence of correctness in the observed cases predicts correctness in this target class. Then apply that defense to the actual judgment, retaining prior evidence and case-specific defeaters. These are not a demand for deductive certainty as the price of induction. They specify what makes an inductive extension warranted. A target-class average cannot automatically override known features making the particular case atypical.

### What source and pooled accuracy actually constrain

The following original hand argument isolates one information requirement; it is not a model of human moral cognition or a reconstruction of the author's complete account. Fix a probability distribution over judgments from two classes. Let the target moral class have weight \(0<\pi\le1\), let \(t_N,t_M\in[0,1]\) be nonmoral and moral correctness rates, and let the pooled rate be

\[
T=(1-\pi)t_N+\pi t_M.
\]

If a warranted premise gives \(T\ge t\), with \(0\le t\le1\), then

\[
\boxed{t_M\ge\max\!\left(0,\frac{t-(1-\pi)}{\pi}\right).}
\]

Since \(t_N\le1\), we have \(T\le1-\pi+\pi t_M\); combining inequalities proves the bound. It is sharp within this unconstrained probability model. For \(0<\pi<1\) and \(t\ge1-\pi\), take \(t_N=1\) and \(t_M=(t-(1-\pi))/\pi\). For \(t<1-\pi\), take \(t_M=0\) and \(t_N=t/(1-\pi)\). At \(\pi=1\), take \(t_M=t\). Each construction attains \(T=t\).

For a simple finite illustration, 99 correct nonmoral judgments and one false moral judgment give 99% pooled accuracy and 0% target accuracy. This is an arithmetic example, not an estimate. More fundamentally, a source rate \(t_N\) alone leaves \(t_M\) unconstrained in this model. Even a perfect source record supplies no nonzero target bound without further information connecting the classes. A pooled premise already covering target judgments is stronger than a source-only record; it cannot be silently substituted for it.

The illustration need not vary normative truth. Hold the target propositions' truth values fixed and vary the procedure's target errors or the target-input distribution while keeping its nonmoral record fixed. The summary then fails to identify target accuracy. This is a limitation of information supplied by that summary, not proof that any assignment of moral truth is metaphysically possible. It does not refute a substantive argument that establishes the additional epistemological predictability. That argument is exactly what would rule out the unconstrained alternatives.

A positive transfer can therefore succeed. A defensible account of the process and its relevant conditions can make source evidence informative about target correctness. Empirical evidence concerning process identity and error mechanisms can contribute; a further epistemic argument must explain its evidential bearing. Simply fitting a free transfer parameter, counting agreement as truth, or defining the reliable class by its preferred conclusions does not provide that warrant. Nor must every successful defense begin with already labeled moral examples: the route under investigation permits substantive theoretical justification of the transfer. Its adequacy remains assessable.

### Fix the experiential property throughout the argument

Street's pain discussion targets the sufferer's pro tanto reason. Her proposed pain term picks out a sensation that is the object of an unreflective negative response; the two may be experientially inseparable. She tests accounts excluding or including that response, while explicitly retaining ordinary relief reasons. Her definition of stance independence also permits attitudes among the facts evaluated. [2006 primary, pp.110–113, 144–152 and notes52–56](https://harjitbhogal.com/Flukes2024/Street%202006%20-%20A%20Darwinian%20dilemma%20for%20realist%20theories%20of%20value.pdf).

Our original response distinguishes two changes. Altering an aversive response may alter the whole experiential episode Q. Altering a judgment about Q's value while holding Q fixed tests whether its evaluative significance depends on endorsing that judgment. These counterfactuals are different; neither their psychological realizability nor their normative conclusion follows merely from stating them. An attitude-involving experience and a truth whose authority is constituted by evaluative endorsement are not the same claim.

A candidate stance-independent conditional could concern the whole episode, including its aversive character: whenever the independently specified Q occurs, it has the proposed disvalue D. Including a response within Q would then describe the object being evaluated; it would not by itself establish that a stance toward the conditional makes it valid. This remains a possible defense to develop, not a refutation of Street's full argument. Her own opening qualification prevents treating any occurrence of an attitude in a ground as automatically fatal to realism. Conversely, independence from one present judgment alone would not meet her stronger conception of stance independence.

This matters directly for reliable access. An account cannot calibrate recognition of a bare sensation, switch to a whole sensation-and-aversion episode in the value claim, and treat the second classification as already validated. Nor can it define Q as whatever is bad and claim to have independently identified the ground. The relevant experience, the judgment formed about it and its purported normative content must stay fixed across the inference.

An independently warranted identity \(Q=D\) remains a complementary positive route. Reliable access to the correctly identified Q could then provide access to D without a second detector. A justified general identity would cover another patient's correctly identified instance; that instance can require fallible testimony or observation without changing the general standard. Establishing the identity and applying it are different tasks. The reliability route above need not assume such an identity, and the identity route need not deny the contribution of empirical evidence.

### Preserve the practical addressee

For the present foundation, distinguish the token experience, its classification, its evaluative significance, identification of the relevant instance in another patient, and that patient's significance as a reason for an actor's response. A successful general account can discharge more than one task, but the claimed discharge needs to be shown. Evidence supporting patient badness does not automatically identify who has which practical reason.

The [parallel unified-grounding account](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/UNIFIED-ETHICAL-GROUNDING.md) supplies the current positive candidate: the patient's burden gives relief its point, while eligible present control connects an actor to that relief. It distinguishes fitting concern, adopting relief as a practical end and choosing effective means. The reliability investigation can support the substantive patient premise and examine the epistemic credentials of the practical principle. It cannot replace that principle with a high accuracy score about sensations. Once practical relevance is warranted, newly identified patients and means can support new applications without re-proving the general ground each time.

Recognition by an actor also differs from the existence of the reason. A learner's justified uncertainty about a patient may affect responsible choice without determining whether the actual episode has disvalue. Likewise, loss of one route to knowledge does not end the patient's condition. These distinctions preserve the earlier correction result and prevent the learner's confidence from becoming the value being explained.

### Which generated claim can carry necessity?

The strongest consequence concerns the architecture of 慧根. The [necessity-transfer argument](HUIGEN-GENERATIVE-CLOSURE.md#h10-whole-interpretation-and-finite-basis-reduction) concerns truth-preserving derivations from an independently justified necessary basis. Induction can provide warranted, fallible acceptance. It does not become truth-preserving deduction merely because a sound foundation licenses its use.

In the mixture example, the mathematical conditional holds throughout the declared model class: whenever its distribution and pooled-bound premises hold, the displayed target bound follows. Actual calibration, sampling and membership remain application premises. Even an established 99% correctness rate does not make each output true, much less necessary. Estimated rates require their own uncertainty treatment. A warranted rate also does not mechanically fix every posterior credence; relevant prior and case-specific evidence still matter.

The whole generative account must therefore preserve the kind of result produced:

| Product | What the inference supports |
|---|---|
| A valid conditional consequence | Its conclusion under its actual premises and interpretation |
| A warranted inductive judgment | Defeasible acceptance at the scope justified by evidence and method |
| A candidate explanation | A proposal requiring further assessment; generation alone supplies no truth certificate |

A necessary root could support all three activities while retaining these distinctions. Its necessarily valid consequences would remain protected by the soundness argument. Its warranted provisional applications could be revised when evidence changes. An account of learning that allows no distinction between these products would mistake correction for contradiction of the foundation itself. Conversely, labeling every failed output provisional after the fact would evade rather than satisfy the claim: the inferential status and revision conditions must be declared when the conclusion is offered.

This does not reduce Ren's objective to mere fallibilism. It specifies the substantive burden of its strongest form: justify the joint root, establish its rules and their scope, and show which claims genuinely inherit necessity. If a practical principle is independently shown necessary, its correctly derived conditional applications can enter that closure. If the present route supplies only defeasible support, that is real epistemic progress but not yet the necessity theorem for the principle. The possibility of a stronger argument remains open.

### Integration and the next discriminating question

This round advances the access bridge by identifying a positive inductive route, its exact transfer obligation and the experiential interpretation it must preserve. The hand bound exposes a hidden inference that could otherwise turn general success into claimed moral reliability. The episode/stance distinction keeps a substantive identity defense available without declaring it established. The typed treatment of generated claims connects both results to the whole-root objective, including learning, concern and wisdom.

The next named connection is **warranted patient disvalue → warranted nonexclusive practical relevance under eligible present control**. Specifically, does the process supporting patient disvalue also support the actor-addressed judgment under a defensible transfer argument, or does that judgment require a further independently justified practical ground? A positive result must identify the actual reason's content, explain the relevance of the actor condition and survive patient-preserving changes of operator. A justified process discontinuity or reason-relevant restriction would narrow the proposed route; a defended common ground would strengthen it. This keeps the central question tied to practical uptake rather than indefinitely demanding more sensation classifiers.

Process individuation, epistemological transfer, patient identification, normative content and permissible application remain supplied where not independently justified. No new controller, formal declaration, empirical validation or inherited scientific rerun is claimed. The accepted conditional foundation stays complete at its declared scope; whole-root necessity remains open.

## 35. Motivational access and the ground of practical reasons

6 October 2026. AI-authored continuation of §34. The named connection is **warranted patient significance → a reason for an unrelated actor → recognition capable of forming new concern**. The previous goal turn was progress: all28 artifact hashes matched the 577-link/324-anchor receipt with55 historical rows. The [primary comparison](HUIGEN-SELF-GROUND-SOURCES.md#32-motivational-access-and-the-ground-of-practical-reasons) tests the strongest motivational restriction instead of treating current indifference as a counterexample. The [accepted conditional foundation](FOUNDATION-ROOT-ACCEPTANCE.md) remains complete at its declared scope.

The current [direct-uptake argument](PRACTICAL-UPTAKE-ARGUMENT.md) already defends a positive, defeasible baseline: an actual patient's relief can favor an eligible stranger's act before that stranger adopts helping as an end. Its modest-cost comparison distinguishes positive justification from permission. The [strongest-foundation formulation](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/STRONGEST-FOUNDATION-CLAIM.md) retains the further demand for a sufficient constitutive ground and faithful coverage of adequate rivals. This round builds on those results; it does not recount their baseline as a new proof.

### The strongest restriction concerns a route, not a present desire

Williams's later reply permits imagination and removal of unconscious obstacles, while requiring a defended distinction between unblocking existing motivations and changing their basis. He challenges substituting an ideal virtuous person's response for the actual actor's reason. Yet note3 treats a successful practical-rational derivation applying whatever the initial motivations as limiting internalism. That opening does not prove such a derivation. [“Replies,” selected pp.186–194 and notes2–8](https://www.scribd.com/document/1065663666/Replies).

This changes the target of disagreement. An actor's lack of an explicit helping intention does not establish absence of every sound route from a wider motivational standpoint. Reflection can reveal implications of commitments, correct mistaken beliefs and develop new concerns. Equally, an intervention that simply causes a new desire need not be sound deliberation. Neither observed refusal nor successful conversion decides which reasons existed before the change.

For our reconstruction let R be the particular patient consideration favoring the actor's eligible relief act, and A be appropriate motivational accessibility through the relevant sound deliberative route. A necessary access condition has the form

\[
R\Rightarrow A.
\]

That implication does not by itself establish its converse, or explain what makes R true. Even a defended equivalence would need an account of explanatory direction before antecedent desires could be declared the ground. Here A concerns responsiveness to a contributory consideration, with relevant competing reasons retained; it is not a guarantee of choosing or performing the act. This is our diagnostic formulation, not an exhaustive formalization of Williams.

### The general wish to act for reasons cannot supply all their content

Consider the proposed common motive: an actor wants to respond to whatever genuinely counts in favor of action. Such a motive can support attention, reflection and uptake. It could help an independently warranted patient-reason generate motivation even when no earlier first-order helping desire was present.

It cannot, by itself, establish which patient facts count in favor. If this general motive is offered as the sole explanation of why this patient's relief is a reason, showing that the act fulfills the motive already requires the disputed practical assessment. The argument uses the patient-reason to identify fulfillment, then uses that fulfillment to establish the patient-reason. It has supplied a transmission route conditional on content, not an independent ground for that content. Another established reason for the same act would not automatically establish that this patient consideration supplies it.

A revised motive, to act on whatever the actor *believes* counts in favor, avoids that semantic dependence. But it changes the explanatory claim. It can explain following a mistaken assessment just as it can explain following a correct one. The assessment's occurrence does not certify the objective patient-ground. This leaves room for justified action under uncertainty and for reasons concerning conscientious deliberation; those require their own account and do not turn every believed consideration into a true patient-directed reason.

A contentful desire to benefit others is a different case. Its relation to actual relief can support a substantive instrumental route. The [earlier overdetermination analysis](#26-patient-reasons-motivational-grounds-and-overdetermination) also allows different motives to support the same reason across different actors. The present result does not eliminate those rivals or infer absence of all motivational support from absence of one helping desire. It identifies the explanatory work that a wholly general reason-following motive cannot perform alone.

The same diagnostic applies to our affirmative account. If recognition of R is used to justify acquiring concern, the concern so generated cannot be the only evidence offered for R. Independent patient-ground warrant, an independently supported contentful route or a defended broader explanatory theory must supply the missing support. Mere mutual implications cannot select a grounded conclusion. This does not reject every form of coherent or mutually supporting justification, adopt a least-fixed-point semantics for ethical truth, or show R false. It blocks a particular unsupported derivation.

### Justify the boundary between correction and a new standpoint

Scanlon compares a failure to understand an existing concern with a failure to recognize a new value, asking why similar imaginative deficiencies should receive different idealization. He preserves subjective conditions on many reasons and separates nonrecognition from irrationality. His closing discussion prefers broader reason attribution but explicitly disclaims an argument forcing agreement. [1998, Appendix363–373](https://www.scribd.com/document/127602229/Scanlon-1998-What-We-Owe-to-Each-Other).

For the generative account, the constructive question is the first transition whose justification matters. Suppose a learner does not yet see why this patient's condition favors relief. One intervention corrects a false belief about the patient's state. Another enables attention to an aspect of the state previously missed. A third installs a desire to comply with a reward. These can all change behavior while having different relationships to the proposed ground. Calling all three learning does not establish their normative equivalence.

A patient-preserving comparison should therefore identify what the actor comes to understand, what earlier limitation is removed, and why the change is warranted. If the actor can recognize that an earlier commitment already bears on the patient, a motivational account can explain that route. If reflection instead supports a newly appreciated reason, the restriction owes an account of why recognition of that content cannot be a sound transition. The affirmative account in turn owes the positive argument that it is recognition rather than projection. An unexamined name such as conversion, correction or rationality settles neither burden.

The important symmetry is a burden of justification, not an automatic identity of cases. Similar errors in interpreting an existing aim and appreciating a new value invite a common explanation, but a substantive difference may justify treating them differently. The relevant standard cannot simply be that every change toward our conclusion repairs a defect and every change away from it corrupts reason. Nor may a proposed restriction define sound deliberation to exclude every substantive revision and then announce that no such revision is possible. These would each build the verdict into the admissibility rule.

### Preserve the actual actor when explaining recognition

Korsgaard distinguishes doubts about practical reason's content from doubts about its motivational reach. She argues that actual nonresponse cannot alone decide content, while retaining a psychological demand on a practical-reason theory. Her discussion does not prove pure practical reason or a categorical ethical principle. [1986, selected pp.12–25](https://studylib.net/doc/8169189/skepticism-about-practical-reason-christine-m.-korsgaard-the).

Our positive account needs both a justified reason and an account of access appropriate to the actual actor. It cannot replace that actor with a perfectly virtuous counterpart, import the counterpart's dispositions and infer an unchanged practical recommendation. Relevant limitations can alter safe means, available options or what can reasonably be demanded. A response appropriate for a skilled, composed helper may be hazardous when attempted by a poorly equipped one. The patient's benefit can remain a source of favor while the particular intervention changes or no permitted effective act is available.

This is compatible with the direct baseline. Its actor is independently specified as informed and able to perform the relevant permissible relief; eligibility is not defined as already caring. The argument concerns what this act would do for this patient through this actor's opportunity. It need not first prescribe becoming an ideal person. Whether an additional deep motivational restriction defeats that reason is the contested issue. Merely supplying an ideal helper or merely reporting the actual actor's refusal addresses a different question.

An actor's inability to recognize a reason also need not imply the patient's lack of standing. The cause of failed uptake, the actor's responsibility for that failure and the available remedy are further judgments. The theory must explain destructive or indifferent regimes as well as constructive ones. Neither universal actual motivation nor universal blame follows from the proposed baseline.

### A common root can have plural routes of recognition

The positive opening is stronger than a stalemate between labels. Williams's limiting case leaves room for a substantive practical principle accessible whatever an admitted rational standpoint's particular motives. A successful version would not need one universally shared first-order helping desire. Different actors could reach the same patient-ground through different justified paths. The target is shared normative content with faithful application, not identical psychology or compulsory assent.

Three achievements must still be distinguished. Showing a path exists for one actor establishes local accessibility. Establishing justified coverage across independently admitted practical standpoints gives a general accessibility result. Establishing that the underlying patient-to-actor principle belongs to a necessary joint basis is stronger still. Neither a universal disposition to believe a proposition nor universal defeasible support makes it necessary. The [sound-generation argument](HUIGEN-GENERATIVE-CLOSURE.md#h10-whole-interpretation-and-finite-basis-reduction) retains its actual premise and validity requirements.

This also clarifies which philosophical disagreement threatens Ren's claim. Accounts named internalist and externalist can preserve the same universal patient reason while differing over the explanation of recognition or the use of the word reason. Such differences do not automatically contradict the necessary content sought by the root. A rival that denies the actual patient-directed favor under the same faithfully interpreted conditions offers a substantive conflict. A rival that retains that favor but limits a narrower notion of motivating or advisory reason requires careful translation, not immediate dismissal. Agreement on a verdict alone still does not establish common grounding.

Scientific competence cannot be silently substituted for full practical understanding. If the necessity claim concerns every adequate scientific philosophy, the account must show why its independently justified standards require preserving this substantive practical relation. Defining an adequate philosophy as one that accepts our ethical conclusion would beg the question. A failure to motivate an actual scientist likewise does not refute a true practical principle. Coverage of the philosophical account and causal influence on its author are different claims.

### Result and next connection

This round supplies an original dependence test for the supposed universal motive, a more discriminating comparison of corrective transitions, and a primary-supported opening for common normative content across different motivational theories. It strengthens the path toward a unified basis by locating the needed entry: a reason can support new concern, but concern generated on that assumption cannot alone prove the reason. The existing direct-uptake judgment remains a defended defeasible result; neither its acceptance nor an access model upgrades it to necessity.

The next named connection is **the actual structure of patient benefit and eligible action → a substantive principle of practical relevance that can justify the first recognition step**. The next comparison should identify which independently justified feature makes relief favor this actor's act, and whether it is preserved across the strongest accounts of sound deliberation. A defended common principle would let the generative account explain plural acquisitions and revisions without making prior concern its foundation. A justified exclusion or demonstrated change of ground would narrow the claim. Repeatedly installing a helping objective would not answer it.

Actual patient identification, practical eligibility, the positive relevance judgment, standards of sound revision and any universal coverage remain supplied where not independently justified. The round adds philosophical analysis and bounded primary comparison, with no new controller, formal declaration, empirical result or scientific rerun. The completed conditional foundation is preserved; necessity of the entire root remains the active objective.

## 36. Can goodness be identified with generativity?

6 October 2026. AI-authored response to [Ren's new question](../../../../sources/15-codex-theory-conversations/2026-10-06-goodness-as-generativity.md): “what is good? can we define good as being generative?” The named connection is **generative realization → a patient's good → the practical significance of enabling it**. This specifies the substantive ground sought in §35 rather than replacing the whole-root objective. The preceding goal turn was progress: all 28 artifact hashes matched the 576-link/316-anchor receipt. The accepted conditional foundation retains its original completed scope.

### A substantive identity is a live hypothesis

We can propose that goodness is constituted by a kind of generative relation. A natural or organizational identification is not ruled out merely because an evaluative term and a causal description have different meanings. The task is to establish the proposed identity and its scope. Declaring that the word good will mean generative settles a usage; it does not yet establish why the identified relation deserves the practical role claimed for goodness.

The question is particularly promising if it seeks the source of value within actual processes rather than an unexplained score imposed on them. An organized being's undergoing, activity, vulnerability and recovery can be real features of that being. A successful account could explain why some transformations constitute benefit, how their significance is recognized, and why that benefit favors an eligible response. It need not treat ethical meaning as forever external to nature. It must also explain the difference between generation that benefits and generation that harms.

The immediate discussion concerns ethical goodness and good-for an affected being. Functional excellence, evidential warrant and aesthetic value are further domains for the whole theory to connect. A good tool can serve an objectionable purpose. An intellectually productive hypothesis can be false. The shared word good cannot establish that these roles have already been reduced to a single quantity.

### Retain the theory's actual meaning of generation

The [formal core](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/FORMAL-CORE.md#definitions-and-conventions) describes a generative effect as producing or reorganizing conditions used by later activity. Regeneration reconstructs a specified support. Boundary, comparison and horizon are explicit. The [governing foundation](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/GENERATIVE-GROUNDING.md#contrasting-regimes-inside-one-explanatory-framework) includes coercive reproduction, mislearning and destruction within the same explanatory account. It does not define all generation as favorable.

Consequently, identifying ethical goodness with this minimal property unchanged would lose an existing distinction. A stipulated coercive organization can reproduce its operators, learn effective repression and renew its resources while burdening the people it controls. These are generative achievements in the declared causal sense. Their existence cannot alone establish ethical merit. Conversely, ending one harmful cycle can improve affected lives. This is the repository's own [regenerative-predator and destructive-rescue challenge](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/ETHICAL-FOUNDATION.md#counterexamples-that-the-unified-theory-must-retain), not a new empirical study or an assertion that no richer identification could work.

Changing the scale can correct a local assessment. What increases a subsystem's reproduction may damage its host or surroundings. But enlarging the boundary does not automatically settle the evaluation: a collective can expand while one member bears a serious loss. The account must retain the affected centers and the distribution of consequences. It cannot replace this work with a scalar count of new structures, copies, options or survivors.

Three candidate claims therefore have different burdens:

| Proposed identification | What it explains or attempts | What remains to establish |
|---|---|---|
| Goodness is any production of later conditions | Includes all minimal generative effects | It fails to discriminate the theory's constructive and destructive cases if their existing ethical assessments stand |
| Goodness is renewal or expansion of a center's capacities | Identifies important supports and developmental possibilities | Which center and capacity, present experiential quality, competing centers, and the value of completion remain material |
| Goodness is constituted by specified beneficial forms of generative realization and support | A live unified explanatory proposal | Beneficial must receive an independently supported identification rather than merely repeat good |

The third formulation is a research hypothesis with unfinished content. The first two cannot be substituted for it after objections arise, and the third cannot claim success merely by absorbing every accepted verdict into its definition.

### Present realization matters as well as future capacity

A definition focused only on producing more future capacity risks overlooking the value of what occurs now. Consider two stipulated histories for the same patient with equal survival, productive capacity, learning and later effective opportunities. In one, a brief unwanted aversive episode is permissibly relieved; in the other it continues. Costs, authority and other affected consequences are matched. If the independently defended local patient-ground applies, the relief can matter even though this selected capacity description does not change.

This is a conceptual comparison with stated premises, not a report of an experimentally demonstrated dissociation or of two physically identical histories. If a proposed realization theory makes the comparison impossible, that requires its own evidence. Nor does the comparison settle every whole-life or attitude-based value theory; those earlier objections remain preserved.

The inherited exact-representation idea gives a precise test. Let G(h) be the proposed generativity description of history h, and J(h) the particular evaluative judgment it claims to determine. If admitted h₀ and h₁ satisfy G(h₀)=G(h₁) but J(h₀)≠J(h₁), no function f can satisfy J(h)=f(G(h)) for both: the same input would require different outputs. This is a direct application of the existing [question-relative representation result](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/FORMAL-CORE.md#f5-faithful-questions-and-the-limits-of-compression), not a new theorem. It tests sufficiency of the declared description, not irreducibility of value to any possible generative account.

Adding the patient's actual experience to G may repair the omission. That would give a richer hypothesis to assess. Its claim that the relevant experiential difference constitutes benefit still needs defense; recording more causal variables does not by itself establish the judgment. Likewise, a welcome rest or completed activity need not purchase its value through later productivity. A theory of generation must make room for realized life as well as further production, without requiring every person to keep generating to merit care.

### A positive proposal centered on differentiated beings

The strongest current formulation is: **some generatively realized conditions constitute goods and harms for particular beings, and some generative transformations realize, restore or support those goods.** This is AI elaboration of a possible route, not Ren's definition or a proof that every value has already been explained.

The concurrent [constitutive candidate](../../../../theories/shengsheng-buxi-generative-existence/unified-2026-10-05/UNIFIED-ETHICAL-GROUNDING.md#the-constitutive-candidate-and-its-test) makes one part concrete. Its C1 identifies a specified still-unwanted experiential whole as locally bad for the actual subject. Its C2 proposes that an eligible act's relief of that burden constitutes a patient-directed moral merit before elective concern. Realization, the experiential domain and practical eligibility remain explicit. This round inherits those defended hypotheses, including their limits, rather than counting them as a new proof. C1 alone does not establish C2, and C2's moral favor does not automatically establish every conception of unrestricted rational authority.

This suggests how generativity could do explanatory work rather than merely rename approval. A proposed process must identify the subject, actual qualitative condition, activity and support relations through which benefit is realized. It must predict when a supposed improvement is only an indicator change, when increased capability damages another center, and when stopping a process answers the ground better than renewing it. A negative reward, forced compliance or growing population is not by itself such an identification. The same requirements apply to a positive signal and an apparently benevolent outcome.

Where this actor already has a justified reason to sustain a patient's continuing good, an eligible effective means of doing so can acquire derivative significance under a warranted practical transmission principle. A renewal, learning or repair process must actually supply the relevant means, with costs and competing claims retained. Mere identification of the patient's good does not establish the actor-addressed premise; this conditional route does not bypass C2. It explains a substantial role for generativity without treating every renewable process as intrinsically good. Whether that patient's good can itself be constituted by a more fundamental generative relation remains the stronger question.

### Primary comparisons sharpen the proposed identity

Thomson argues that benefit to another can favor action even when the actor does not care, while distinguishing that favor from motivation and what ought to be done. Her examples also separate benefit to a lawn from benefit to its owner; further discussion distinguishes human welfare, organism health and artifact function. Her account supplies an affirmative comparator, not a demonstrated zero-reason rival. [Goodness and Advice, selected Part I and II](https://dokumen.pub/goodness-and-advice-core-textbooknbsped-9781400824724.html).

Darwall locates the force of welfare reasons in the person's worthiness of care, rather than the existence of a caring attitude. That permits reasons before recognition, but the worthiness claim is substantive. His distinction between welfare and what a person rationally values permits genuine sacrifice and blocks a simple identification with successful pursuit. [Welfare and Rational Care, selected opening](https://dokumen.pub/welfare-and-rational-care-course-booknbsped-9781400825325.html).

Hurka questions whether care can explain welfare without already depending on judgments about it. He accepts agent-neutral reasons to care while challenging the proposed grounding and the demand that every caring episode contain an explicit universal value judgment. That combination preserves a substantive patient-ground inquiry without equating actual caring with philosophical endorsement. [“A Kantian Theory of Welfare?”, §§1–3](https://thomashurka.com/wp-content/uploads/2014/02/a-kantian-theory-of-welfare.pdf).

Our use of these comparisons is limited. They help identify whose condition matters, the direction of explanation and the difference between a ground and its recognition.
